### [2026-05-31T20:54:39Z] risk_analyst (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from risk_analyst

Risk register for the BRC event-pump + durable memory refactor (issue #2908). 14 risks identified across correctness, durability, performance, and operator-hard-requirement categories. Verdict ACK on the plan-as-framed (Option B from refine + cq-1/2/3/4 HITL resolutions) with the three highest-severity risks tracked: R1 cq-3 durability gap (existing state_store async best-effort push + in-memory health-monitor anchors do NOT meet the operator hard requirement that no-progress safety budget + parked HITL decisions survive SDLC-host failure — plan MUST extend), R2 Qwen-route TTL ceiling (cross-session cache survives 5.5min idle measured, but ~15min idle vs real 10-13min BRC idles is unmeasured — WS0 spike must instrument), R5 memory shape (Open Decision #1 append-only vs distill is load-bearing for cache cost AND orient-don't-constrain per agent-mode-design.md — plan MUST commit). Eleven additional medium / low risks documented including R3 cursor /tmp loss across pod restart, R4 gateway credential expiry on long idles, R6 mcp/cli drift test break when net-new CLI verbs land without MCP retirement per cq-1, R7 prior-fix preservation across BRC preamble collapse (#2323/#2064/#2482/#1995/#2036/#2451/#2142/#2725), R8 cq-4 no-flag old-wrapper deletion cutover risk, R9 WS3 next-action endpoint scope (must cover lifecycle decoding incl. dual-role producer-first #2749 + open-NACK aggregation #2142), R11 claude --print regression risk vs the refine-phase primitive correction, R12 metadata-not-content delta shape, R14 SSE silent-failure observability. Runtime-primitive audit identifies the 5 net-new CLI commands required (brc get-state, brc list-blocking, phase get-context, brc resolve-obligation, brc read-peer-artifact) with handler parity already in place — only the CLI wrappers are missing. Trust-boundary audit flags cq-3 as a known violation that the plan MUST remediate. Rollback plan documented per-workstream; WS1 + WS4 are coordinated-rollback paths, others individually revert-safe. blocking_concerns is empty — this is my producer artifact; reviewer verdicts on architect + task_planner will populate the field when those producers propose. Satisfies the plan-phase risk_analyst task: assess technical risks, identify runtime-primitive and trust-boundary gaps per #2594, surface human review areas, propose rollback. Tests: dry-run JSON parse + key-presence check (verdict=ACK, 14 risks, 3 in top_3, empty blocking_concerns).

### [2026-05-31T20:57:19Z] reviewer_plan → risk_analyst (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_plan for risk_analyst

Reviewed the 14-risk register in 2908-risk_analyst-output.json against the refine analysis, all four HITL cq-* resolutions, and spot-verified file:line evidence. The register is comprehensive, well-evidenced, and correctly traces every HIGH-severity risk to either explicit operator hard-requirements (cq-2/3/4) or named anti-patterns (agent-mode-design.md). ACK with one non-blocking observation for the planner.

### Verification of cited file:line evidence

- **R1 / state_store.py:719-740 + 800-928**: confirmed. `save_pipeline()` writes via temp-file+rename then `_commit_state` → `_sync_to_remote_async()` (line 805 area). The "best-effort async push to remote after every commit" comment is right there in the code; the synchronous boundary truly does close before durable remote crossing. The cq-3 hard requirement ("must be robust to SDLC-host failure ... persisted DURABLY SERVER-SIDE") is genuinely not met by this primitive today.
- **R1 / health_monitor.py:82-103**: confirmed. `AgentState` dataclass is entirely in-memory (`last_heartbeat`, `last_progress`, `last_activity`, escalation flags, `last_progress_data` dict are all field defaults). No on-disk persistence, no startup_reconciliation hook.
- **R2 / patch_litellm_cache.py:104-152**: confirmed. The Patch 2 block widens cache_control gate to qwen/deepseek but the file's reasoning is correct — egg only unstrips OpenRouter native cache_control; TTL is provider-side and unmeasured at the 15-min idle horizon.
- **R3 / orch_cli.py:1377+ cursor**: confirmed (`base = os.environ.get("EGG_WAIT_CURSOR_DIR", "/tmp")` at line ~1426). Risk's claim that /tmp is wiped on pod restart is correct; risk's mitigation correctly notes that as long as the wrapper invokes `egg-orch message wait-loop` (vs rolling its own wait), the existing since_id_stale handling preserves correctness.
- **handlers/brc.py:679 brc_get_state / :726 brc_list_blocking / :743 brc_resolve_obligation / :901 brc_read_peer_artifact**: all confirmed at the cited line ranges.

### Strengths

- **Cross-references the HITL resolutions, not just the analysis prose.** R1 cites cq-3's "durable server-side state" hard requirement verbatim. R2 cites cq-2's "instrument-not-cap + suppression during AWAITING_HUMAN" verbatim. R5 cites cq-2-Q2's "ephemeral memory is acceptable ONLY because recovery must NOT depend on it" hard caveat. R8 cites cq-4's "no flag" override and correctly flags that it directly contradicts the analysis Constraints section line 178-180 — that contradiction matters for the planner.
- **Each risk has a primitive_existence_check and a trust_boundary_check** keyed to the relevant audit rubric (criteria §9, §10). R1's "BOUNDARY VIOLATION — the orchestrator returns success to the agent before the durable write has actually crossed the host boundary" is exactly the right framing for cq-3.
- **R9 (next-action endpoint scope) is the right scope-creep flag.** The endpoint must encode dual-role producer-first (#2749), open-NACK aggregation (#2142), conditional-ACK lifecycle (#1998), stale-version rejection (#2482), resolve_obligation (#2338). Risk correctly identifies that WS6 prompt collapse is partially blocked on WS3 completion — that's a real ordering constraint the task_planner will need to honour.
- **R11 (claude --print regression) catches a live trap.** The issue body itself still says "SDK-native one-shot claude -p" while the refine analysis explicitly corrects this. A coder reading the issue body as authoritative could re-introduce the EGG100 anti-pattern; risk correctly mandates the WS1 task name `python3 -m egg_agent` and cites docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90-104.
- **R12 (memory content baking) and R5 (memory shape decision) together** correctly frame Open Decision #1 against BOTH the cache axis AND the orient-don't-constrain axis from agent-mode-design.md. The refine analysis flagged this at line 360-367; risk operationalises it as a NACK trigger for the architect ("if architect leaves this open, NACK").
- **R6 (test surface drift) catches a CI break the architect/task_planner would miss.** test_mcp_cli_drift.py:264-290 hard-asserts `actual_gaps == expected_gaps`; test_server.py:110 hard-codes `TOOL_LIST` count 31. Any new CLI verb shipped under cq-1 without updating both will fail CI. Risk correctly budgets this into WS3/WS8-net-new task scope.
- **rollback_plan_per_workstream is per-workstream not monolithic** and correctly notes WS1's no-flag deletion (cq-4) is the highest-effort rollback path. WS4 (liveness migration) is flagged as the second-hardest because the in-agent heartbeat daemon must be kept deletable-but-not-deleted until rollback window closes — that's a specific implementation constraint the coder will need.

### Non-blocking

- **R6 evidence — `egg-orch consensus status` already wraps `brc_get_state`.** orch_cli.py:2783 `cmd_consensus_status` calls `_handlers.brc_get_state(req)` and supports slice scoping via `--slice-id`. The risk register's "brc get-state CLI — NOT YET BUILT" framing is technically right under the WS3 nomenclature (no `egg-orch brc get-state` verb exists), but functionally the slice-aware get-state is already CLI-reachable via `egg-orch consensus status --json [--slice-id S]`. The task_planner should clarify WS3 scope: is it adding a new `brc` subcommand namespace (CLI parity for the MCP namespace), or adding the *missing* verbs (`list-blocking`, `phase get-context`, `next-action`, `resolve-obligation`, `read-peer-artifact`) under existing subcommands? The latter is smaller; the former is the cq-1 "guarantee CLI availability" requirement read literally.
- **R14 OVERSEER_ALERT trigger** for N consecutive SSE-timeout-without-event cycles is a useful suggestion but the threshold ("e.g. 5x the expected event interval") needs operator calibration. Suggest the planner surface this as a configurable rather than hardcoding a multiplier.
- The "verdict": "ACK" field at the top of the JSON is meta-commentary on the plan-as-framed before architect and task_planner have proposed; it could be misread as binding. Suggest the risk_analyst's prompt clarify that the risk register itself is the deliverable and the verdict belongs to reviewer_plan, not the producer. (Not a fault of this proposal — convention concern only.)
- R13 (cross-role memory reads) flags a "needs_validation" item — confirm via shared/egg_restrictions/patterns.py that cross-role *reads* are permitted (write allowlists are confirmed; the read side of the in-pod tool interceptor was not verified). If reads are blocked, the architect must adjust memory location plan.

````yaml

      \ suppression during AWAITING_HUMAN\" verbatim. R5 cites cq-2-Q2's \"ephemeral\
      \ memory is acceptable ONLY because recovery must NOT depend on it\" hard caveat.\
      \ R8 cites cq-4's \"no flag\" override and correctly flags that it directly\
      \ contradicts the analysis Constraints section line 178-180 \u2014 that contradiction\
      \ matters for the planner.\n- **Each risk has a primitive_existence_check and\
      \ a trust_boundary_check** keyed to the relevant audit rubric (criteria \xA7\
      9, \xA710). R1's \"BOUNDARY VIOLATION \u2014 the orchestrator returns success\
      \ to the agent before the durable write has actually crossed the host boundary\"\
      \ is exactly the right framing for cq-3.\n- **R9 (next-action endpoint scope)\
      \ is the right scope-creep flag.** The endpoint must encode dual-role producer-first\
      \ (#2749), open-NACK aggregation (#2142), conditional-ACK lifecycle (#1998),\
      \ stale-version rejection (#2482), resolve_obligation (#2338). Risk correctly\
      \ identifies that WS6 prompt collapse is partially blocked on WS3 completion\
      \ \u2014 that's a real ordering constraint the task_planner will need to honour.\n\
      - **R11 (claude --print regression) catches a live trap.** The issue body itself\
      \ still says \"SDK-native one-shot claude -p\" while the refine analysis explicitly\
      \ corrects this. A coder reading the issue body as authoritative could re-introduce\
      \ the EGG100 anti-pattern; risk correctly mandates the WS1 task name `python3\
      \ -m egg_agent` and cites docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90-104.\n- **R12\
      \ (memory content baking) and R5 (memory shape decision) together** correctly\
      \ frame Open Decision #1 against BOTH the cache axis AND the orient-don't-constrain\
      \ axis from agent-mode-design.md. The refine analysis flagged this at line 360-367;\
      \ risk operationalises it as a NACK trigger for the architect (\"if architect\
      \ leaves this open, NACK\").\n- **R6 (test surface drift) catches a CI break\
      \ the architect/task_planner would miss.** test_mcp_cli_drift.py:264-290 hard-asserts\
      \ `actual_gaps == expected_gaps`; test_server.py:110 hard-codes `TOOL_LIST`\
      \ count 31. Any new CLI verb shipped under cq-1 without updating both will fail\
      \ CI. Risk correctly budgets this into WS3/WS8-net-new task scope.\n- **rollback_plan_per_workstream\
      \ is per-workstream not monolithic** and correctly notes WS1's no-flag deletion\
      \ (cq-4) is the highest-effort rollback path. WS4 (liveness migration) is flagged\
      \ as the second-hardest because the in-agent heartbeat daemon must be kept deletable-but-not-deleted\
      \ until rollback window closes \u2014 that's a specific implementation constraint\
      \ the coder will need.\n\n### Non-blocking\n\n- **R6 evidence \u2014 `egg-orch\
      \ consensus status` already wraps `brc_get_state`.** orch_cli.py:2783 `cmd_consensus_status`\
      \ calls `_handlers.brc_get_state(req)` and supports slice scoping via `--slice-id`.\
      \ The risk register's \"brc get-state CLI \u2014 NOT YET BUILT\" framing is\
      \ technically right under the WS3 nomenclature (no `egg-orch brc get-state`\
      \ verb exists), but functionally the slice-aware get-state is already CLI-reachable\
      \ via `egg-orch consensus status --json [--slice-id S]`. The task_planner should\
      \ clarify WS3 scope: is it adding a new `brc` subcommand namespace (CLI parity\
      \ for the MCP namespace), or adding the *missing* verbs (`list-blocking`, `phase\
      \ get-context`, `next-action`, `resolve-obligation`, `read-peer-artifact`) under\
      \ existing subcommands? The latter is smaller; the former is the cq-1 \"guarantee\
      \ CLI availability\" requirement read literally.\n- **R14 OVERSEER_ALERT trigger**\
      \ for N consecutive SSE-timeout-without-event cycles is a useful suggestion\
      \ but the threshold (\"e.g. 5x the expected event interval\") needs operator\
      \ calibration. Suggest the planner surface this as a configurable rather than\
      \ hardcoding a multiplier.\n- The \"verdict\": \"ACK\" field at the top of the\
      \ JSON is meta-commentary on the plan-as-framed before architect and task_planner\
      \ have proposed; it could be misread as binding. Suggest the risk_analyst's\
      \ prompt clarify that the risk register itself is the deliverable and the verdict\
      \ belongs to reviewer_plan, not the producer. (Not a fault of this proposal\
      \ \u2014 convention concern only.)\n- R13 (cross-role memory reads) flags a\
      \ \"needs_validation\" item \u2014 confirm via shared/egg_restrictions/patterns.py\
      \ that cross-role *reads* are permitted (write allowlists are confirmed; the\
      \ read side of the in-pod tool interceptor was not verified). If reads are blocked,\
      \ the architect must adjust memory location plan."
````

### [2026-05-31T21:01:07Z] architect (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from architect

Architecture analysis for #2908 (BRC event-pump + durable agent memory). Recommends Option A: stateless event-pump wrapper + per-role memory (`.egg-state/agent-outputs/<role>/brc-memory.md`) + server-side next-action endpoint; SDK-native `claude -p` per event; legacy capped-restart wrapper deleted in-issue per cq-4. Rejects prompt-only (B), custom streaming harness (C), and flagged-legacy-fallback (D). 6-slice forest DAG (slice-1 root, linear chain 1→2→3→4, slice-5 and slice-6 siblings under slice-4): (1) WS0 de-risking spike + Qwen TTL measurement on #2906 repro; (2) `consensus next-action` endpoint + net-new CLI verbs (`brc list-blocking`, `phase get-context`) + durable HITL/no-progress-budget persistence schema (cq-3 hard requirement); (3) `brc-memory.md` artifact + `brc_ack`/`brc_nack` handler memory-write scaffolding (ephemeral per refine Q2); (4) event-pump consensus_wrapper rewrite + wrapper-side liveness migration (WS1+WS4) + safety-budget consumer + legacy deletion (cq-4); (5) prompt collapse + delta-scoped re-analysis; (6) Qwen-route bounded keep-warm (conditional on slice-1 data). Runtime-primitive assumptions surfaced with file:line evidence per #2594 across 11 primitives (wrapper template, message_wait_loop, MAX_CONSENSUS_RESTARTS, heartbeat ticker, _build_brc_preamble, durable persistence, file-write boundary, MCP tools, schema migrator, message wait-loop CLI). Out of scope per cq-1: collapse of 28 agent-facing MCP tools (deferred to follow-up). Hard constraints captured: cq-2 (Qwen on new pump directly, instrument-only thresholds), cq-3 (durable server-side persistence survives host failure), cq-4 (delete old path entirely, no flagged fallback), WS8 prose-via-stdin rule (#2741 regression guard).

### [2026-05-31T21:04:14Z] risk_analyst → architect (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from risk_analyst for architect


Reviewed the architect's plan through the risk lens (R1 cq-3 durability gap, R11 claude --print regression in particular). Found 2 blocking issues that would surface as expensive implement-phase NACKs or production data loss if shipped as-framed, plus 2 non-blocking clarifications. **Both blocking issues are direct failures of refine-phase / HITL-resolution invariants that the architect's plan inherits but does not respect.**

### Blocking

1. **2908-architect-output.json:7,10,14,15,31,45,52,85,118,126,166,174,209,220,276,351 + slices.yaml:8,87,132 — REGRESSION of the refine-phase primitive correction. The plan uses `claude -p` as the per-event invocation primitive throughout (14 occurrences in the JSON, 3 in the slices.yaml, ZERO occurrences of `python3 -m egg_agent` or `build_agent_command`).** This is the EGG100-linted anti-pattern (docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90-104) that the refine-phase analysis EXPLICITLY corrected at .egg-state/drafts/2908-analysis.md:41-58 ("The issue text refers to SDK-native one-shot `claude -p`. That phrasing is incorrect for egg: `claude --print` is an EGG100-linted anti-pattern... because it exits after one response and cannot use tools or handle multi-turn work"). The correct primitive — `python3 -m egg_agent` via `egg_agent.build_agent_command()` — is **already in production in the very file the plan rewrites** at orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:748-759 with the explicit comment "Uses the Agent SDK entry point instead of the claude CLI." The architect's plan even cites consensus_wrapper.py:716 (build_consensus_wrapped_command) as the entry the new wrapper template lives in, but specifies the invocation inside as `claude -p`, which directly contradicts what build_consensus_wrapped_command emits today. **Fix**: replace every `claude -p` reference (summary, recommended_approach.name, why_recommended[1], slice-1/3/4 summaries, all runtime_primitive_assumptions entries, constraint #8, dependencies_external[0], risks_for_risk_analyst[5], out_of_scope_explicitly[8], and slices.yaml lines 8/87/132) with the actual primitive: `python3 -m egg_agent` invoked via `egg_agent.build_agent_command()`. The "SDK-native one-shot" framing is preserved — `python3 -m egg_agent` is itself one-shot (exits when the agent reaches its stop state) — but it correctly wires MCP, tool permissions, EGG_AGENT_ROLE scoping, and tool_interceptor checks into a single process, which `claude --print` cannot. Without this fix, the coder will hit EGG100 lint on their first commit and the work cannot land. The refine-phase analysis already did this correction once; the plan must not regress it.

2. **2908-architect-output.json:16 + runtime_primitive_assumptions[5] (lines 152-158) — cq-3 durability HARD requirement is NOT satisfied by the proposed mechanism.** The plan claims: "the parked HITL decision + the no-progress safety-budget state are written to the orchestrator's contract store (.egg-state/contracts/<pid>.json + branch-committed via the existing schemaVersion 1.2 migrator pattern), not held in the host process. On host death + restart, a new host re-reads the contract state and resumes from the parked decision and budget without losing either." This claim is **factually wrong** about the durability properties of the underlying primitive. Evidence: orchestrator/state_store.py:11 ("async push via a daemon thread"), state_store.py:804-805 ("Best-effort async push to remote after every commit" → `self._sync_to_remote_async()`), state_store.py:890-928 (debounced daemon-thread push), state_store.py:127 (`_push_in_flight: ClassVar[bool]`). orchestrator/contract_store.py:139-142 (load_contract_from_branch docstring) confirms the failure mode: "Tries `origin/<branch>` first — the remote ref survives local cleanup and reflects the **last pushed state** — then falls back to `<branch>` in case the main repo has a local ref but no origin copy (**e.g. pre-push crashes**)." Combined with orchestrator-deployment.yaml's emptyDir-backed /home and .egg-state volumes (which my pre-review analysis confirmed; pod restart wipes them), the sequence is: save_pipeline() writes locally and returns success → async push thread is scheduled → host crashes 50ms later before the push fires → pod restarts with empty emptyDir → load_contract_from_branch finds no origin ref and no local ref → returns None → cq-3 hard requirement violated. cq-3's text is explicit: "the parked HITL decision AND the safety-budget / no-progress state must be persisted DURABLY SERVER-SIDE in the orchestrator, not held only in the host process. If the host running the SDLC pipeline dies, a restarted host must be able to resume the awaiting-human decision and the budget state rather than losing them. Treat host-death recoverability as a requirement of this decision, not a nice-to-have." This is a known-class data-loss surface for the SOLE safety net under cq-4 (which deletes the old wrapper entirely — there is no fallback). **Fix**: slice-2 must specify (a) a synchronous-flush variant of save_pipeline() / commit-and-push that returns only after origin ref reflects the write, used specifically for HITL-decision writes and safety-budget transition writes (the cost is on the order of a remote push latency, acceptable for these rare mutations); OR (b) a different durable backing store for the safety-budget + parked-HITL state (e.g. Redis with AOF persistence, Postgres, a server-side blocking HTTP gateway endpoint that fsyncs before returning); AND (c) extend startup_reconciliation to explicitly replay safety-budget state from the durable store rather than initialising-to-zero (the existing in-memory health_monitor.py:82-103 anchors regress on every restart per my R1 evidence). slice-4 integration test in the architect's risks_for_risk_analyst[1] mitigation already calls for "inject a synthetic host-kill mid-budget and verify a fresh host resumes from the persisted state" — that test cannot pass against the current best-effort-async-push primitive. Without the primitive change, the test catches the bug post-implement, which is the expensive late catch this NACK is preventing.

### Non-blocking

- **slices.yaml — slice-2 depends_on slice-1 contradicts the narrative.** Slice-1's text says "Findings shape slice-2 next-action endpoint and slice-6 cache decisions but do not block slice-2/3/4/5 design — those proceed in parallel to the spike where possible." But the depends_on field has slice-2 depending on slice-1. Either the dependency is hard (and slice-2 cannot start until slice-1 completes) or soft (and slice-1 only informs slice-2's design choices but does not block start). Clarify whether the spike-data input to slice-2's next-action endpoint shape (od-3 decision rule) is at design-time or at land-time. If soft, change depends_on to empty + add a note that slice-1 informs but does not block. If hard, drop the "proceed in parallel" sentence from slice-1's narrative.

- **runtime_primitive_assumptions[5] / slice-3 brc-memory.md location vs role-subdir convention.** The plan says ".egg-state/agent-outputs/<role>/brc-memory.md" with "<role>/" subdir convention NEW in slice-3, citing patterns.py:362-484 as already covering the prefix. Verify: do the existing role allowlists cover `<role>/` subdirectories under `.egg-state/agent-outputs/`, or only the flat prefix? My pre-review noted "every role can write .egg-state/agent-outputs/" but did not separately verify the subdir-vs-flat permission. If the patterns are prefix-glob (`.egg-state/agent-outputs/**`), the subdir is fine; if the patterns are flat-path-only, slice-3 silently fails the tool_interceptor check on first memory write. This is a 30-second confirmation against the patterns module — should be done in slice-3 before the file format is wired.

- **risks_for_risk_analyst[5] R5 mitigation contract.** "If slice-4 lands and slice-5 NACKs on prompt-vs-wrapper drift, the contract is: revert slice-5 only, slice-4 keeps shipping." This is correct in principle but the BRC preamble's stay-alive instructions are read by EVERY pre-slice-5 agent in production — if slice-4 ships with the preamble unchanged (per slice-4's "drop stay-alive only" minimal edit), agents may still try to wait_loop because other parts of the preamble (the pre-confirm-wait foot-gun, cursor-threading instructions) reference the wait. Confirm that slice-4's "minimal stay-alive edit" actually breaks the agent's behaviour back to event-handler shape without slice-5's full collapse, or document the operational interval where prompts and wrapper are partially-migrated as a known transient.

### Out of risk-analyst scope but worth flagging to the architect for completeness

The architect's own risks list does not call out R7 (prior-fix preservation across BRC preamble collapse — #2323/#2064/#2482/#1995/#2036/#2451/#2142/#2725) at the same scope my risk register did. The architect's plan touches `_build_brc_preamble` in slice-5 (full collapse) and slice-4 (minimal edit); the prior-fix lineage is embedded in the preamble text. The plan should enumerate which prior-fix instructions survive the collapse by (a) orchestrator-side enforcement (safe), (b) CLI-level enforcement (safe if wrapper uses CLI), or (c) prompt-instruction-only (REQUIRES preservation). Not a NACK condition for the architect — task_planner will encode the prior-fix preservation gates as test acceptance criteria — but the architect's plan should mention the audit step exists.

Three blocking items + several non-blocking observations.

### Blocking

1. **Wrong primitive throughout — `claude -p` instead of `python3 -m egg_agent` (criteria §1 Alignment, §9 Primitive-Existence Audit).**

   The plan names `claude -p` as the per-event invocation in 8+ places:
   - `summary` (line 7): "on each actionable event it invokes `claude -p` (SDK-native one-shot, no custom harness)"
   - `recommended_approach.name` (line 10): "SDK-native `claude -p` per event"
   - `slices[0].name` (slice-1): "minimal throwaway wrapper that loops `claude -p` per BRC event"
   - `slices[2].name` (slice-3): "continuity between per-event `claude -p` invocations"
   - `slices[3].name` (slice-4): "invokes `claude -p` per actionable event"
   - `slices[3].summary` (slice-4): "invokes `claude -p` per actionable event"
   - `dependencies_external[0]`: "`claude -p` per-event invocation is the SDK-native one-shot"
   - `open_decisions_to_resolve_during_implementation` od-2: same primitive

   This is the **EGG100-linted anti-pattern**, documented at docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:89-102 verbatim:

   > "LLM calls must never use direct `httpx.post()` or Anthropic SDK calls, and must not invoke `claude --print` as a subprocess. ... `claude --print` is not an approved invocation path — it exits after one response and cannot use tools or handle multi-turn workflows. Use `build_agent_command()` for container-spawned agents..."

   The refine analysis at .egg-state/drafts/2908-analysis.md lines 41-58 explicitly identified this as a **"Primitive correction relative to the issue body"** and called out exactly this regression:

   > "The issue text refers to 'SDK-native one-shot `claude -p`'. That phrasing is incorrect for egg: `claude --print` is an EGG100-linted anti-pattern (`docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90–104`) because it exits after one response and cannot use tools or handle multi-turn work. ... The stateless-event-pump *shape* is unaffected: `python3 -m egg_agent` is already one-shot (it exits when the agent reaches its stop state) and wires MCP, tool permissions, `EGG_AGENT_ROLE` scoping, and the tool-interceptor checks into a single process."

   And the refine analysis runtime-primitives table at line 327 explicitly lists:
   > "Agent SDK entry point `python3 -m egg_agent` (built by `egg_agent.build_agent_command()`) | `shared/egg_agent/`; **already invoked by `orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:748–759`** | orchestrator-spawned containers. **NOT** `claude --print` (EGG100 anti-pattern per `docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90–104`)"

   I just verified this anchor: `consensus_wrapper.py:716-770` builds the agent command via `agent_prefix_parts = ["python3", "-m", "egg_agent", ...]` with the explicit in-code comment "Uses the Agent SDK entry point instead of the claude CLI." `shared/egg_agent/command.py:11-46` is `build_agent_command()` and its docstring states "The orchestrator spawns containers that run the Agent SDK via `python3 -m egg_agent`. ... the entry point wraps the Agent SDK (`claude_agent_sdk.query()`), so CLI-specific flags like `--print` and `--dangerously-skip-permissions` are not needed."

   R11 in the just-ACKed risk register flagged this exact regression with HIGH likelihood and the architect committed it:

   > "Risk that implementation regresses the refine-phase fix and re-introduces 'claude -p' / 'claude --print' as the per-event invocation. ... Risk: a coder reading the issue body's 'claude -p' phrasing as authoritative could re-introduce the anti-pattern; the refine analysis is the authoritative correction. Plan MUST cite docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90-104 in the wrapper-migration task description and require coder to use build_agent_command() / python3 -m egg_agent (NOT claude --print)."

   Per criteria §9: the primitive `claude -p` exists but in a different form than the plan assumes — it's an EGG100-linted anti-pattern, not the supported one-shot path for long-running tool-using agents. The correct primitive `python3 -m egg_agent` (built by `build_agent_command()`) exists at the exact `file:line` the architect already cites as evidence (consensus_wrapper.py:716).

   **Fix**: Replace every `claude -p` reference in the plan (summary, recommended_approach, slice-1/3/4/6 names + goals, dependencies_external, open_decisions od-2) with `python3 -m egg_agent` or "the Agent SDK entry point via `egg_agent.build_agent_command()`". Cite `consensus_wrapper.py:716-770` as the already-present invocation pattern the wrapper rewrite preserves. Cite `docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:89-102` as the EGG100 constraint the coder must honour. The architecture *shape* (stateless event-pump + per-event invocation) is unchanged; only the primitive name needs correcting.

2. **Memory shape decision left open (criteria §5 Risk, §7 Completeness; risk register R5).**

   slice-3's goal says: "Memory shape (append-only vs rewrite-and-distill — open decision #1 in the issue body) is decided in this slice against the spike findings from slice-1: if the Qwen-route cache survives long enough for delta analysis to dominate prefix-cache cost, choose rewrite-and-distill (bounded growth); otherwise append-only with a tail compaction."

   That is a **decision rule**, not a decision. It punts the choice to slice-3 against slice-1 data. The just-ACKed risk register R5 stated explicitly:

   > "Plan MUST commit to a memory shape with explicit rationale evaluated against BOTH axes: (a) cache cost (does the shape burst the per-event prefix?) AND (b) orient-don't-constrain (does the shape constrain the agent's attention as it grows?). The refine-phase analysis flags this — the plan should not leave it open. ... if architect leaves this open, NACK."

   The refine analysis at lines 360-367 makes the same point with a default: "an unbounded append-only memory eventually starts *constraining* what the per-event agent can attend to (sea-of-context), while a distilled memory keeps 'small summaries that orient' as the target shape. The decision should be made against both axes, not just token cost."

   The architect's rule weighs only the cache axis. The orient-don't-constrain axis is missing from the decision criterion.

   **Fix**: Commit slice-3 to **distilled / rewrite-and-distill** as the default per the refine analysis ("distilled memory keeps small summaries that orient as the target shape"). Cite both axes in the rationale: (a) cache cost — distilled bursts the prefix on each rewrite, but the rewrite is bounded by a section template so the burst is small + predictable; (b) orient-don't-constrain — distilled summaries orient without flooding the per-event agent's attention. If slice-1's WS0 measurement shows distilled busts cache materially on Qwen, slice-3 may add an append-only tail with compaction — but the *default* is distilled and the decision is made now, not deferred.

3. **slice-2 conflates three distinct file-categories — orchestrator route + sandbox CLI + contract schema (criteria §11 Slice Sizing — softer than slice-4 but still a sizing concern).**

   slice-2 bundles: (a) `consensus next-action --role R` HTTP endpoint on the orchestrator (`orchestrator/routes/*`), (b) net-new CLI verbs (`sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py`, `sandbox/egg_lib/contract_cli.py`), (c) durable persistence schema (`.egg-state/contracts/<pid>.json` schema additions via `orchestrator/contract_store.py` migrator + `shared/egg_contracts/*` Pydantic models). That's **three distinct file-categories** with different reviewer surfaces:
   - orchestrator/routes/ wants reviewer_code attention to route registration, lifecycle decoding, dual-role producer-first ordering (#2749), open-NACK barrier (#2142)
   - sandbox/egg_lib/ wants attention to _handler_dispatch parity vs MCP, prose-via-stdin/file rule (#2741)
   - shared/egg_contracts/ + orchestrator/contract_store/ wants attention to schemaVersion bump migrator + default-value backwards-compat for in-flight contracts (cq-3 host-restart recovery)

   Per criteria §11: "A single slice touches more than ~3 distinct file-categories ... probably wants subdivision." The risk register R9 explicitly notes WS3's next-action endpoint scope is "underspecified" and lifecycle decoding (dual-role producer-first, open-NACK barrier, conditional ACK, stale-version re-review, resolve_obligation) is non-trivial. Combining that with brand-new CLI verbs and a contract schema migration in a single BRC cycle invites independent NACK surfaces from different reviewer concerns.

   **Fix**: Subdivide along the orchestrator/sandbox/contract seams:
   - **slice-2a**: cq-3 durable persistence schema only — `pipeline.no_progress_budget` field + parked-HITL state on `.egg-state/contracts/<pid>.json` via schemaVersion migrator. Default values preserve current behavior; no consumers wired. Smallest, lowest-risk; lands first.
   - **slice-2b**: next-action HTTP endpoint on orchestrator (or extension of `consensus status --json` per od-3). Pure additive route work. Depends on 2a if it surfaces budget state in the next-action payload.
   - **slice-2c**: net-new CLI verbs (`brc list-blocking`, `phase get-context`, `brc next-action` if 2b takes the new-verb path). Pure additive sandbox/egg_lib work, all using `_handler_dispatch` for parity. Depends on 2b for the next-action verb.

   This also opens parallelism: 2a can ship while 2b is in BRC; 2c blocks on 2b but is small. Each is one-reviewer-surface.

### Non-blocking

- **slice-4 sizing**: slice-4 bundles WS1 wrapper rewrite + WS4 heartbeat migration + cq-3 safety-budget consumer + SSE consumer deletion + concurrent_executor.py rewire + minimal prompt edit + old wrapper deletion (MAX_CONSENSUS_RESTARTS, _RECOVERY_SYSTEM_PROMPT, _RECOVERY_USER_PROMPT). The architect themselves anticipates this: "This slice has the largest blast radius in the issue; the BRC cycle will likely need to subdivide between the orchestrator side and the deletion sweep if the producer hits >3 NACK rounds, but the contract is intentionally one slice because cq-4 forbids retaining the old path through any intermediate state." Per criteria §11 final paragraph, the architect cites a deliberate override (cq-4 atomicity) and the rationale is on the record — so this is NOT a hard NACK. However, the cq-4 reading is strict: cq-4's "no flagged fallback" is about FINAL state, not intermediate slice structure. If implement-time experience shows >3 NACK rounds, consider an in-slice subdivision: (4a) wrapper-side heartbeat CLI verb (additive — adds the verb without removing the agent's existing daemon); (4b) wrapper template rewrite + consumer rewire + deletion sweep — both ship within slice-4's PR but get separate BRC review surfaces. Operator override on this sizing is fine if it's deliberate; I am flagging not blocking.

- **Dependency over-serialization**: slice-1 description says spike findings "do not block slice-2/3/4/5 design — those proceed in parallel to the spike where possible." Yet `slices[1].depends_on = ["slice-1"]` serializes slice-2 behind slice-1. Same for slice-3 → slice-2 → slice-1. If slice-2 (CLI/endpoint/schema) and slice-3 (memory file format) are truly design-independent of slice-1 (spike), make those dependencies explicit by removing them or replacing with `informed_by` / `validated_against` annotations the task_planner / scheduler treats as soft. As-drafted, slices 2/3/4/5 all wait for slice-1 to clear BRC before any of them can start, even though their design doesn't need slice-1's numbers.

- **patterns.py:362-484 line-range cited for allowlist coverage is incomplete**: covers ARCHITECT (362), TASK_PLANNER (372), RISK_ANALYST (382), REVIEWER_AGENT_DESIGN (479-484), but NOT REFINER (488-507), REVIEWER_REFINE (509-514), or REVIEWER_PLAN (516+). The actual point — every BRC-participating role's allowlist covers `.egg-state/agent-outputs/` — is correct; the cited range just doesn't span all the roles. Tighten the citation to "every role allowlist in patterns.py:362-516+ includes `.egg-state/agent-outputs/`".

- **slice-3 brc-memory.md write via handler must use the dict-arg path, not argv**: slice-3 says brc_ack/brc_nack handler appends a structured entry to memory using `reason` + `files_reviewed` from the agent. Verify that the handler receives `reason` via dict argument (memory) not via argv — argv routing reintroduces the #2741 Bash shell-metachar corruption (covered in the WS8 constraint at constraints[6] but worth re-noting on the slice-3 handler-scaffolding task description).

- **Risk register R3 (cursor /tmp loss) is partially mooted but worth a slice-4 task-level note**: existing `egg-orch message wait-loop` CLI handles `since_id_stale` server-side and uses `EGG_WAIT_CURSOR_DIR` (default `/tmp`) for cursor persistence. As long as slice-4's wrapper invokes the existing CLI (which it does per the architect's slice-4 description), cursor handling is preserved. Slice-4 task description should state explicitly "wrapper invokes `egg-orch message wait-loop` CLI, does NOT roll its own wait/cursor logic" — to forestall a coder shortcut that bypasses the CLI and loses the #2323 fix.

- **R8 (old-wrapper deletion + cq-4 no-flag cutover) is correctly identified as a migration risk in the risk register**: slice-4 task description should include the cutover procedure (drain in-flight pipelines before deploying the slice-4 PR) so the coder/tester have a concrete operational handle. The architect's open_decisions od-4 covers the safety budget shape but not the cutover playbook; add it.

### Restate the path forward

Three blocking items: (1) replace `claude -p` with `python3 -m egg_agent` / `build_agent_command()` throughout the plan, (2) commit slice-3 to a memory shape (default distilled) with both-axis rationale, (3) subdivide slice-2 along the schema/endpoint/CLI seam. Address those, re-propose. Slice-4 sizing and the dependency-serialization are non-blocking — flag for awareness, not required for re-ACK.

````yaml
      \ \u2014 is correct; the cited range just doesn't span all the roles. Tighten\
      \ the citation to \"every role allowlist in patterns.py:362-516+ includes `.egg-state/agent-outputs/`\"\
      .\n\n- **slice-3 brc-memory.md write via handler must use the dict-arg path,\
      \ not argv**: slice-3 says brc_ack/brc_nack handler appends a structured entry\
      \ to memory using `reason` + `files_reviewed` from the agent. Verify that the\
      \ handler receives `reason` via dict argument (memory) not via argv \u2014 argv\
      \ routing reintroduces the #2741 Bash shell-metachar corruption (covered in\
      \ the WS8 constraint at constraints[6] but worth re-noting on the slice-3 handler-scaffolding\
      \ task description).\n\n- **Risk register R3 (cursor /tmp loss) is partially\
      \ mooted but worth a slice-4 task-level note**: existing `egg-orch message wait-loop`\
      \ CLI handles `since_id_stale` server-side and uses `EGG_WAIT_CURSOR_DIR` (default\
      \ `/tmp`) for cursor persistence. As long as slice-4's wrapper invokes the existing\
      \ CLI (which it does per the architect's slice-4 description), cursor handling\
      \ is preserved. Slice-4 task description should state explicitly \"wrapper invokes\
      \ `egg-orch message wait-loop` CLI, does NOT roll its own wait/cursor logic\"\
      \ \u2014 to forestall a coder shortcut that bypasses the CLI and loses the #2323\
      \ fix.\n\n- **R8 (old-wrapper deletion + cq-4 no-flag cutover) is correctly\
      \ identified as a migration risk in the risk register**: slice-4 task description\
      \ should include the cutover procedure (drain in-flight pipelines before deploying\
      \ the slice-4 PR) so the coder/tester have a concrete operational handle. The\
      \ architect's open_decisions od-4 covers the safety budget shape but not the\
      \ cutover playbook; add it.\n\n### Restate the path forward\n\nThree blocking\
      \ items: (1) replace `claude -p` with `python3 -m egg_agent` / `build_agent_command()`\
      \ throughout the plan, (2) commit slice-3 to a memory shape (default distilled)\
      \ with both-axis rationale, (3) subdivide slice-2 along the schema/endpoint/CLI\
      \ seam. Address those, re-propose. Slice-4 sizing and the dependency-serialization\
      \ are non-blocking \u2014 flag for awareness, not required for re-ACK."

  reason: "Reviewed 2908-architect-output.json (380-line JSON) and 2908-architect-slices.yaml\
    \ (6-slice scaffold) against the refine analysis at .egg-state/drafts/2908-analysis.md,\
    \ all four cq-* HITL resolutions, the risk register at .egg-state/agent-outputs/2908-risk_analyst-output.json\
    \ (just ACKed), and verified the cited primitives in consensus_wrapper.py, shared/egg_agent/command.py,\
    \ docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md, and shared/egg_restrictions/patterns.py.\n\
    \nThree blocking items + several non-blocking observations.\n\n### Blocking\n\n\
    1. **Wrong primitive throughout \u2014 `claude -p` instead of `python3 -m egg_agent`\
    \ (criteria \xA71 Alignment, \xA79 Primitive-Existence Audit).**\n\n   The plan\
    \ names `claude -p` as the per-event invocation in 8+ places:\n   - `summary`\
    \ (line 7): \"on each actionable event it invokes `claude -p` (SDK-native one-shot,\
    \ no custom harness)\"\n   - `recommended_approach.name` (line 10): \"SDK-native\
    \ `claude -p` per event\"\n   - `slices[0].name` (slice-1): \"minimal throwaway\
    \ wrapper that loops `claude -p` per BRC event\"\n   - `slices[2].name` (slice-3):\
    \ \"continuity between per-event `claude -p` invocations\"\n   - `slices[3].name`\
    \ (slice-4): \"invokes `claude -p` per actionable event\"\n   - `slices[3].summary`\
    \ (slice-4): \"invokes `claude -p` per actionable event\"\n   - `dependencies_external[0]`:\
    \ \"`claude -p` per-event invocation is the SDK-native one-shot\"\n   - `open_decisions_to_resolve_during_implementation`\
    \ od-2: same primitive\n\n   This is the **EGG100-linted anti-pattern**, documented\
    \ at docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:89-102 verbatim:\n\n   > \"LLM calls must\
    \ never use direct `httpx.post()` or Anthropic SDK calls, and must not invoke\
    \ `claude --print` as a subprocess. ... `claude --print` is not an approved invocation\
    \ path \u2014 it exits after one response and cannot use tools or handle multi-turn\
    \ workflows. Use `build_agent_command()` for container-spawned agents...\"\n\n\
    \   The refine analysis at .egg-state/drafts/2908-analysis.md lines 41-58 explicitly\
    \ identified this as a **\"Primitive correction relative to the issue body\"**\
    \ and called out exactly this regression:\n\n   > \"The issue text refers to 'SDK-native\
    \ one-shot `claude -p`'. That phrasing is incorrect for egg: `claude --print`\
    \ is an EGG100-linted anti-pattern (`docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90\u2013\
    104`) because it exits after one response and cannot use tools or handle multi-turn\
    \ work. ... The stateless-event-pump *shape* is unaffected: `python3 -m egg_agent`\
    \ is already one-shot (it exits when the agent reaches its stop state) and wires\
    \ MCP, tool permissions, `EGG_AGENT_ROLE` scoping, and the tool-interceptor checks\
    \ into a single process.\"\n\n   And the refine analysis runtime-primitives table\
    \ at line 327 explicitly lists:\n   > \"Agent SDK entry point `python3 -m egg_agent`\
    \ (built by `egg_agent.build_agent_command()`) | `shared/egg_agent/`; **already\
    \ invoked by `orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:748\u2013759`** | orchestrator-spawned\
    \ containers. **NOT** `claude --print` (EGG100 anti-pattern per `docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90\u2013\
    104`)\"\n\n   I just verified this anchor: `consensus_wrapper.py:716-770` builds\
    \ the agent command via `agent_prefix_parts = [\"python3\", \"-m\", \"egg_agent\"\
    , ...]` with the explicit in-code comment \"Uses the Agent SDK entry point instead\
    \ of the claude CLI.\" `shared/egg_agent/command.py:11-46` is `build_agent_command()`\
    \ and its docstring states \"The orchestrator spawns containers that run the Agent\
    \ SDK via `python3 -m egg_agent`. ... the entry point wraps the Agent SDK (`claude_agent_sdk.query()`),\
    \ so CLI-specific flags like `--print` and `--dangerously-skip-permissions` are\
    \ not needed.\"\n\n   R11 in the just-ACKed risk register flagged this exact regression\
    \ with HIGH likelihood and the architect committed it:\n\n   > \"Risk that implementation\
    \ regresses the refine-phase fix and re-introduces 'claude -p' / 'claude --print'\
    \ as the per-event invocation. ... Risk: a coder reading the issue body's 'claude\
    \ -p' phrasing as authoritative could re-introduce the anti-pattern; the refine\
    \ analysis is the authoritative correction. Plan MUST cite docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90-104\
    \ in the wrapper-migration task description and require coder to use build_agent_command()\
    \ / python3 -m egg_agent (NOT claude --print).\"\n\n   Per criteria \xA79: the\
    \ primitive `claude -p` exists but in a different form than the plan assumes \u2014\
    \ it's an EGG100-linted anti-pattern, not the supported one-shot path for long-running\
    \ tool-using agents. The correct primitive `python3 -m egg_agent` (built by `build_agent_command()`)\
    \ exists at the exact `file:line` the architect already cites as evidence (consensus_wrapper.py:716).\n\
    \n   **Fix**: Replace every `claude -p` reference in the plan (summary, recommended_approach,\
    \ slice-1/3/4/6 names + goals, dependencies_external, open_decisions od-2) with\
    \ `python3 -m egg_agent` or \"the Agent SDK entry point via `egg_agent.build_agent_command()`\"\
    . Cite `consensus_wrapper.py:716-770` as the already-present invocation pattern\
    \ the wrapper rewrite preserves. Cite `docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:89-102`\
    \ as the EGG100 constraint the coder must honour. The architecture *shape* (stateless\
    \ event-pump + per-event invocation) is unchanged; only the primitive name needs\
    \ correcting.\n\n2. **Memory shape decision left open (criteria \xA75 Risk, \xA7\
    7 Completeness; risk register R5).**\n\n   slice-3's goal says: \"Memory shape\
    \ (append-only vs rewrite-and-distill \u2014 open decision #1 in the issue body)\
    \ is decided in this slice against the spike findings from slice-1: if the Qwen-route\
    \ cache survives long enough for delta analysis to dominate prefix-cache cost,\
    \ choose rewrite-and-distill (bounded growth); otherwise append-only with a tail\
    \ compaction.\"\n\n   That is a **decision rule**, not a decision. It punts the\
    \ choice to slice-3 against slice-1 data. The just-ACKed risk register R5 stated\
    \ explicitly:\n\n   > \"Plan MUST commit to a memory shape with explicit rationale\
    \ evaluated against BOTH axes: (a) cache cost (does the shape burst the per-event\
    \ prefix?) AND (b) orient-don't-constrain (does the shape constrain the agent's\
    \ attention as it grows?). The refine-phase analysis flags this \u2014 the plan\
    \ should not leave it open. ... if architect leaves this open, NACK.\"\n\n   The\
    \ refine analysis at lines 360-367 makes the same point with a default: \"an unbounded\
    \ append-only memory eventually starts *constraining* what the per-event agent\
    \ can attend to (sea-of-context), while a distilled memory keeps 'small summaries\
    \ that orient' as the target shape. The decision should be made against both axes,\
    \ not just token cost.\"\n\n   The architect's rule weighs only the cache axis.\
    \ The orient-don't-constrain axis is missing from the decision criterion.\n\n\
    \   **Fix**: Commit slice-3 to **distilled / rewrite-and-distill** as the default\
    \ per the refine analysis (\"distilled memory keeps small summaries that orient\
    \ as the target shape\"). Cite both axes in the rationale: (a) cache cost \u2014\
    \ distilled bursts the prefix on each rewrite, but the rewrite is bounded by a\
    \ section template so the burst is small + predictable; (b) orient-don't-constrain\
    \ \u2014 distilled summaries orient without flooding the per-event agent's attention.\
    \ If slice-1's WS0 measurement shows distilled busts cache materially on Qwen,\
    \ slice-3 may add an append-only tail with compaction \u2014 but the *default*\
    \ is distilled and the decision is made now, not deferred.\n\n3. **slice-2 conflates\
    \ three distinct file-categories \u2014 orchestrator route + sandbox CLI + contract\
    \ schema (criteria \xA711 Slice Sizing \u2014 softer than slice-4 but still a\
    \ sizing concern).**\n\n   slice-2 bundles: (a) `consensus next-action --role\
    \ R` HTTP endpoint on the orchestrator (`orchestrator/routes/*`), (b) net-new\
    \ CLI verbs (`sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py`, `sandbox/egg_lib/contract_cli.py`),\
    \ (c) durable persistence schema (`.egg-state/contracts/<pid>.json` schema additions\
    \ via `orchestrator/contract_store.py` migrator + `shared/egg_contracts/*` Pydantic\
    \ models). That's **three distinct file-categories** with different reviewer surfaces:\n\
    \   - orchestrator/routes/ wants reviewer_code attention to route registration,\
    \ lifecycle decoding, dual-role producer-first ordering (#2749), open-NACK barrier\
    \ (#2142)\n   - sandbox/egg_lib/ wants attention to _handler_dispatch parity vs\
    \ MCP, prose-via-stdin/file rule (#2741)\n   - shared/egg_contracts/ + orchestrator/contract_store/\
    \ wants attention to schemaVersion bump migrator + default-value backwards-compat\
    \ for in-flight contracts (cq-3 host-restart recovery)\n\n   Per criteria \xA7\
    11: \"A single slice touches more than ~3 distinct file-categories ... probably\
    \ wants subdivision.\" The risk register R9 explicitly notes WS3's next-action\
    \ endpoint scope is \"underspecified\" and lifecycle decoding (dual-role producer-first,\
    \ open-NACK barrier, conditional ACK, stale-version re-review, resolve_obligation)\
    \ is non-trivial. Combining that with brand-new CLI verbs and a contract schema\
    \ migration in a single BRC cycle invites independent NACK surfaces from different\
    \ reviewer concerns.\n\n   **Fix**: Subdivide along the orchestrator/sandbox/contract\
    \ seams:\n   - **slice-2a**: cq-3 durable persistence schema only \u2014 `pipeline.no_progress_budget`\
    \ field + parked-HITL state on `.egg-state/contracts/<pid>.json` via schemaVersion\
    \ migrator. Default values preserve current behavior; no consumers wired. Smallest,\
    \ lowest-risk; lands first.\n   - **slice-2b**: next-action HTTP endpoint on orchestrator\
    \ (or extension of `consensus status --json` per od-3). Pure additive route work.\
    \ Depends on 2a if it surfaces budget state in the next-action payload.\n   -\
    \ **slice-2c**: net-new CLI verbs (`brc list-blocking`, `phase get-context`, `brc\
    \ next-action` if 2b takes the new-verb path). Pure additive sandbox/egg_lib work,\
    \ all using `_handler_dispatch` for parity. Depends on 2b for the next-action\
    \ verb.\n\n   This also opens parallelism: 2a can ship while 2b is in BRC; 2c\
    \ blocks on 2b but is small. Each is one-reviewer-surface.\n\n### Non-blocking\n\
    \n- **slice-4 sizing**: slice-4 bundles WS1 wrapper rewrite + WS4 heartbeat migration\
    \ + cq-3 safety-budget consumer + SSE consumer deletion + concurrent_executor.py\
    \ rewire + minimal prompt edit + old wrapper deletion (MAX_CONSENSUS_RESTARTS,\
    \ _RECOVERY_SYSTEM_PROMPT, _RECOVERY_USER_PROMPT). The architect themselves anticipates\
    \ this: \"This slice has the largest blast radius in the issue; the BRC cycle\
    \ will likely need to subdivide between the orchestrator side and the deletion\
    \ sweep if the producer hits >3 NACK rounds, but the contract is intentionally\
    \ one slice because cq-4 forbids retaining the old path through any intermediate\
    \ state.\" Per criteria \xA711 final paragraph, the architect cites a deliberate\
    \ override (cq-4 atomicity) and the rationale is on the record \u2014 so this\
    \ is NOT a hard NACK. However, the cq-4 reading is strict: cq-4's \"no flagged\
    \ fallback\" is about FINAL state, not intermediate slice structure. If implement-time\
    \ experience shows >3 NACK rounds, consider an in-slice subdivision: (4a) wrapper-side\
    \ heartbeat CLI verb (additive \u2014 adds the verb without removing the agent's\
    \ existing daemon); (4b) wrapper template rewrite + consumer rewire + deletion\
    \ sweep \u2014 both ship within slice-4's PR but get separate BRC review surfaces.\
    \ Operator override on this sizing is fine if it's deliberate; I am flagging not\
    \ blocking.\n\n- **Dependency over-serialization**: slice-1 description says spike\
    \ findings \"do not block slice-2/3/4/5 design \u2014 those proceed in parallel\
    \ to the spike where possible.\" Yet `slices[1].depends_on = [\"slice-1\"]` serializes\
    \ slice-2 behind slice-1. Same for slice-3 \u2192 slice-2 \u2192 slice-1. If slice-2\
    \ (CLI/endpoint/schema) and slice-3 (memory file format) are truly design-independent\
    \ of slice-1 (spike), make those dependencies explicit by removing them or replacing\
    \ with `informed_by` / `validated_against` annotations the task_planner / scheduler\
    \ treats as soft. As-drafted, slices 2/3/4/5 all wait for slice-1 to clear BRC\
    \ before any of them can start, even though their design doesn't need slice-1's\
    \ numbers.\n\n- **patterns.py:362-484 line-range cited for allowlist coverage\
    \ is incomplete**: covers ARCHITECT (362), TASK_PLANNER (372), RISK_ANALYST (382),\
    \ REVIEWER_AGENT_DESIGN (479-484), but NOT REFINER (488-507), REVIEWER_REFINE\
    \ (509-514), or REVIEWER_PLAN (516+). The actual point \u2014 every BRC-participating\
    \ role's allowlist covers `.egg-state/agent-outputs/` \u2014 is correct; the cited\
    \ range just doesn't span all the roles. Tighten the citation to \"every role\
    \ allowlist in patterns.py:362-516+ includes `.egg-state/agent-outputs/`\".\n\n\
    - **slice-3 brc-memory.md write via handler must use the dict-arg path, not argv**:\
    \ slice-3 says brc_ack/brc_nack handler appends a structured entry to memory using\
    \ `reason` + `files_reviewed` from the agent. Verify that the handler receives\

    \ the #2741 Bash shell-metachar corruption (covered in the WS8 constraint at constraints[6]\
    \ but worth re-noting on the slice-3 handler-scaffolding task description).\n\n\
    - **Risk register R3 (cursor /tmp loss) is partially mooted but worth a slice-4\
    \ task-level note**: existing `egg-orch message wait-loop` CLI handles `since_id_stale`\
    \ server-side and uses `EGG_WAIT_CURSOR_DIR` (default `/tmp`) for cursor persistence.\
    \ As long as slice-4's wrapper invokes the existing CLI (which it does per the\
    \ architect's slice-4 description), cursor handling is preserved. Slice-4 task\
    \ description should state explicitly \"wrapper invokes `egg-orch message wait-loop`\
    \ CLI, does NOT roll its own wait/cursor logic\" \u2014 to forestall a coder shortcut\
    \ that bypasses the CLI and loses the #2323 fix.\n\n- **R8 (old-wrapper deletion\
    \ + cq-4 no-flag cutover) is correctly identified as a migration risk in the risk\
    \ register**: slice-4 task description should include the cutover procedure (drain\
    \ in-flight pipelines before deploying the slice-4 PR) so the coder/tester have\
    \ a concrete operational handle. The architect's open_decisions od-4 covers the\
    \ safety budget shape but not the cutover playbook; add it.\n\n### Restate the\

    \ -m egg_agent` / `build_agent_command()` throughout the plan, (2) commit slice-3\
````

### [2026-05-31T21:16:44Z] architect (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from architect

v2 of architect output for #2908 addressing both reviewer NACKs. Blocking fixes: (1) replaced every `claude -p` reference with `python3 -m egg_agent` invoked via `build_agent_command()` — the SDK entry point already in production at orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:748-759. `claude --print` is the EGG100-linted anti-pattern per docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90-104. Refine analysis at .egg-state/drafts/2908-analysis.md:41-58 explicitly corrected the issue body's incorrect phrasing; v1 missed that correction. (2) Spelled out the cq-3 durability fix: NEW `commit_and_push_sync` synchronous-flush variant of save_pipeline that returns ONLY after `git push origin <branch>` completes + NEW `_startup_reconciliation_replay_safety_budget` step that primes the in-memory health_monitor.py:82-103 anchors from durable storage on host boot. The existing best-effort-async-push primitive (state_store.py:11/:804-805/:890-928) does NOT meet cq-3 on its own; contract_store.py:139-142 confirms 'pre-push crashes' failure mode. (3) Subdivided v1 slice-2 along the orchestrator-route / sandbox-CLI / contract-schema seams into v2 slices 2 (durable persistence schema + sync-flush + reconciliation), 3 (next-action HTTP endpoint), 4 (net-new CLI verbs). (4) Committed v2 slice-5 (was v1 slice-3) memory shape to distilled / rewrite-and-distill DEFAULT per refine analysis lines 360-367, with rationale on BOTH axes (cache cost AND orient-don't-constrain per agent-mode-design.md). Non-blocking addressed: patterns.py citation tightened to span all BRC-participating roles (362-516+) + matchers.py:33 prefix-glob evidence for subdir coverage; explicit dict-arg handler path in slice-5 (preserves #2741); slice-6 uses existing `egg-orch message wait-loop` CLI (preserves #2323); slice-6 cutover playbook (drain in-flight before deploy); slice-7 prior-fix preservation audit (#2323/#2064/#2482/#1995/#2036/#2451/#2142/#2725); R5 mitigation contract clarity. Slice DAG grows from 6 to 8 slices; forest constraint preserved (linear chain 1→2→3→4→5→6, slices 7 and 8 are siblings under 6).

**Adversarial re-review**

**Your v2 review has TWO equal-weight mandates:**

1. **Verify named v1 blockers were addressed** — confirm the producer fixed what you NACK'd.
2. **Audit the delta since your last review (per REVIEWER-SYNC.md: `git log {last_reviewed_commit}..HEAD --not origin/{base_branch} -p` — the commits landed since the version you last verdicted, NOT the whole accumulated proposal surface) as a fresh reviewer** — ignore your v1 NACK history. Read that diff as if you'd never seen the prior version. Apply your lens (security threat-model, concurrency races, contract AC, line-by-line bugs, silent-fallback shapes — whichever your role owns) to the delta itself, not to whether your previous concerns were satisfied. **Mandate 2 is bounded to this delta** — it does NOT ask you to re-traverse the whole accumulated surface from earlier cycles; that work was amortized when you first reviewed those commits.

Both mandates have equal weight. If (1) passes but (2) finds new issues, you NACK. ACK requires both pass.

**The named-blockers anchor is a known trap. Every reviewer lens has a mandate-2 in its own territory** — security has newly-introduced threat surfaces, concurrency has newly-introduced races, contract has newly-introduced AC drift, code has newly-introduced line-by-line bugs. The four issues that escaped PR #2724 to the GitHub bot were all of code-lens shape (`${ANSWER}` as bare Python, deprecated `datetime.utcnow()`, non-atomic write, bare `except: pass`) — the persistent reviewer correctly answered mandate 1 ("did prior issues get fixed? yes") and skipped mandate 2 ("does this delta introduce new issues? actually yes"). The shape generalizes: whatever your lens, this delta can introduce issues your prior NACK didn't name. Watching the producer deliver a targeted fix pulls strongly toward "verify my fix-request landed → ACK." Recognize the pull and do mandate 2 anyway.

**How to execute mandate 2:**

- Read each new hunk as an operator who's about to copy-paste / run / integrate it. Would this code execute as written? Would these docs send a copy-paster down a working path?
- Apply every rubric pass to the new hunks. New issues outside the scope of your prior NACK are blocking; your prior NACK does not bound this re-review.
- **Fresh-reviewer simulation.** Before issuing your v2 verdict, ask: would a reviewer who has only seen this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK history ACK this? If you can't argue yes from that diff alone, NACK.
- **External-bot anchor.** Imagine `egg-reviewer[bot]` reads only this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK context. What would it flag? Anything it'd flag, you should NACK first.

**Your v2 verdict must enumerate both halves** so mandate 2 doesn't silently disappear from the record:

- (a) Which v1 blockers you verified-fixed (mandate 1).
- (b) What new issues you audited-and-did-not-find (mandate 2). Name the specific shapes you checked — not "reviewed thoroughly," but "checked for silent fallbacks, doc-snippet executability, API-deprecation, atomicity of file writes." If you can't enumerate (b), you haven't done mandate 2.

**Re-reviews are cheap by design.** Your amortized context means the work is "read the delta, apply your rubric, decide" — minutes, not hours. NACK without hesitance; the orchestrator absorbs cycles. Two NACKs on the same producer where the second names new findings is the correct trajectory, not goalpost-moving. The downstream GitHub reviewer should find nothing in this delta. Anything it catches that lives in this cycle's diff is a miss attributable to this re-review.


### [2026-05-31T21:17:15Z] architect (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from architect

v2 of architect output for #2908 addressing both reviewer NACKs. Blocking fixes: (1) replaced every `claude -p` reference with `python3 -m egg_agent` invoked via `build_agent_command()` — the SDK entry point already in production at orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:748-759. `claude --print` is the EGG100-linted anti-pattern per docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90-104. Refine analysis at .egg-state/drafts/2908-analysis.md:41-58 explicitly corrected the issue body's incorrect phrasing; v1 missed that correction. (2) Spelled out the cq-3 durability fix: NEW `commit_and_push_sync` synchronous-flush variant of save_pipeline that returns ONLY after `git push origin <branch>` completes + NEW `_startup_reconciliation_replay_safety_budget` step that primes the in-memory health_monitor.py:82-103 anchors from durable storage on host boot. The existing best-effort-async-push primitive (state_store.py:11/:804-805/:890-928) does NOT meet cq-3 on its own; contract_store.py:139-142 confirms 'pre-push crashes' failure mode. (3) Subdivided v1 slice-2 along the orchestrator-route / sandbox-CLI / contract-schema seams into v2 slices 2 (durable persistence schema + sync-flush + reconciliation), 3 (next-action HTTP endpoint), 4 (net-new CLI verbs). (4) Committed v2 slice-5 (was v1 slice-3) memory shape to distilled / rewrite-and-distill DEFAULT per refine analysis lines 360-367, with rationale on BOTH axes (cache cost AND orient-don't-constrain per agent-mode-design.md). Non-blocking addressed: patterns.py citation tightened to span all BRC-participating roles (362-516+) + matchers.py:33 prefix-glob evidence for subdir coverage; explicit dict-arg handler path in slice-5 (preserves #2741); slice-6 uses existing `egg-orch message wait-loop` CLI (preserves #2323); slice-6 cutover playbook (drain in-flight before deploy); slice-7 prior-fix preservation audit (#2323/#2064/#2482/#1995/#2036/#2451/#2142/#2725); R5 mitigation contract clarity. Slice DAG grows from 6 to 8 slices; forest constraint preserved (linear chain 1→2→3→4→5→6, slices 7 and 8 are siblings under 6).

**Adversarial re-review**

**Your v3 review has TWO equal-weight mandates:**

1. **Verify named v2 blockers were addressed** — confirm the producer fixed what you NACK'd.
2. **Audit the delta since your last review (per REVIEWER-SYNC.md: `git log {last_reviewed_commit}..HEAD --not origin/{base_branch} -p` — the commits landed since the version you last verdicted, NOT the whole accumulated proposal surface) as a fresh reviewer** — ignore your v2 NACK history. Read that diff as if you'd never seen the prior version. Apply your lens (security threat-model, concurrency races, contract AC, line-by-line bugs, silent-fallback shapes — whichever your role owns) to the delta itself, not to whether your previous concerns were satisfied. **Mandate 2 is bounded to this delta** — it does NOT ask you to re-traverse the whole accumulated surface from earlier cycles; that work was amortized when you first reviewed those commits.

Both mandates have equal weight. If (1) passes but (2) finds new issues, you NACK. ACK requires both pass.

**The named-blockers anchor is a known trap. Every reviewer lens has a mandate-2 in its own territory** — security has newly-introduced threat surfaces, concurrency has newly-introduced races, contract has newly-introduced AC drift, code has newly-introduced line-by-line bugs. The four issues that escaped PR #2724 to the GitHub bot were all of code-lens shape (`${ANSWER}` as bare Python, deprecated `datetime.utcnow()`, non-atomic write, bare `except: pass`) — the persistent reviewer correctly answered mandate 1 ("did prior issues get fixed? yes") and skipped mandate 2 ("does this delta introduce new issues? actually yes"). The shape generalizes: whatever your lens, this delta can introduce issues your prior NACK didn't name. Watching the producer deliver a targeted fix pulls strongly toward "verify my fix-request landed → ACK." Recognize the pull and do mandate 2 anyway.

**How to execute mandate 2:**

- Read each new hunk as an operator who's about to copy-paste / run / integrate it. Would this code execute as written? Would these docs send a copy-paster down a working path?
- Apply every rubric pass to the new hunks. New issues outside the scope of your prior NACK are blocking; your prior NACK does not bound this re-review.
- **Fresh-reviewer simulation.** Before issuing your v3 verdict, ask: would a reviewer who has only seen this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK history ACK this? If you can't argue yes from that diff alone, NACK.
- **External-bot anchor.** Imagine `egg-reviewer[bot]` reads only this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK context. What would it flag? Anything it'd flag, you should NACK first.

**Your v3 verdict must enumerate both halves** so mandate 2 doesn't silently disappear from the record:

- (a) Which v2 blockers you verified-fixed (mandate 1).
- (b) What new issues you audited-and-did-not-find (mandate 2). Name the specific shapes you checked — not "reviewed thoroughly," but "checked for silent fallbacks, doc-snippet executability, API-deprecation, atomicity of file writes." If you can't enumerate (b), you haven't done mandate 2.

**Re-reviews are cheap by design.** Your amortized context means the work is "read the delta, apply your rubric, decide" — minutes, not hours. NACK without hesitance; the orchestrator absorbs cycles. Two NACKs on the same producer where the second names new findings is the correct trajectory, not goalpost-moving. The downstream GitHub reviewer should find nothing in this delta. Anything it catches that lives in this cycle's diff is a miss attributable to this re-review.

### [2026-05-31T21:17:45Z] risk_analyst → task_planner (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from risk_analyst for task_planner


Reviewed `.egg-state/drafts/2908-plan.md` (877 lines) against my risk register and the parallel architect-NACK in flight from this role. The plan has materially improved on several risk-lens findings compared to the architect's plan, but inherits one HIGH-severity blocking gap from the upstream architect plan that the task-level structure does not address.

### What the plan got right (acknowledge before listing the blocker)

- **R11 `claude --print` regression — FIXED in the authoritative prose.** The plan body uses `python3 -m egg_agent` via `build_agent_command` throughout (lines 12-13, 74, 83, 148, 205, 270, 286, 614, 630-632, 688), and TASK-4-3 has an explicit acceptance criterion: `grep -n 'claude --print\|claude -p\b'` against `orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py` returns zero hits. TASK-1-1 even applies the rule to the throwaway spike script. Excellent.
- **R6 drift-test break — addressed.** TASK-2-12 explicitly updates `tests/tools/test_mcp_cli_drift.py` for the five new CLI verbs; each CLI shim uses `_handler_dispatch` to share handlers with the MCP tool (TASK-2-2 through TASK-2-5).
- **R7 prior-fix preservation — addressed by name.** TASK-4-10 explicitly names fix-lineage assertions by issue number: cursor threading (#2323), gateway keep-alive (#2451), open-NACK barrier surfaced through next-action (#2142), pre-confirm-wait rejection (#2064/#2482), gap-race (#1995). Direct response to my R7 concern.
- **R12 metadata-not-content delta — addressed.** TASK-5-3 and TASK-5-4 explicitly require the endpoint to return paths only (never inlined contents); TASK-5-4 docstring "MUST NOT fetch file contents — that is the agent's job via git tools."
- **#2741 stdin/file-path rule — addressed.** TASK-2-9 explicitly tests "if any of the new CLI verbs accept prose-bearing arguments (e.g. `resolve-obligation --note`), the test confirms the input takes `--note-file` or stdin rather than argv."
- **Trust-boundary fixture correction (better than my risk register).** TASK-4-11 explicitly mandates `integration_tests/local_pipeline/test_event_pump_qwen_repro.py` location for fixture reachability, citing `integration_tests/local_pipeline/conftest.py:261` (gateway_url fixture) vs `integration_tests/conftest.py:78` (EggStack.gateway_url attribute, NOT a fixture). This is a #2474/#2594-class trust-boundary call-out that the architect's plan did not surface; net-add of correctness.
- **Cost-callback path correction (also better than my risk register).** TASK-1-3 explicitly identifies that the refine-phase analysis's `~/.local/state/clm/cost-*.json` reference does NOT match the current code — the LiteLLM cost callback at `config/litellm/cost_callback.py` emits structured log lines (no on-disk JSON files in the agent pod). Net-add of correctness.

### Blocking

1. **2908-plan.md TASK-2-6 / TASK-2-7 / TASK-2-10 (lines 396-413, 433-438) + TASK-4-9 (lines 678-685) — INHERITS the architect's cq-3 durability gap. The plan specifies "the existing schemaVersion migrator pattern" as the durability mechanism for `pipeline.no_progress_budget` + `pipeline.parked_hitl`, but the underlying store (orchestrator/state_store.py:11 docstring "async push via a daemon thread"; state_store.py:804-805 "Best-effort async push to remote after every commit" → `_sync_to_remote_async()`; state_store.py:890-928 debounced daemon-thread push) does NOT satisfy the cq-3 HARD requirement that the state survive SDLC-host failure.** This is the same R1 issue the parallel architect NACK already names; task_planner's TASK-2-10 test simulates a different (and weaker) failure mode than the production reality.

Specifically, TASK-2-10 says: "The test simulates SDLC-host death (writer process exits; new reader process opens **the same contract file**) and asserts both fields round-trip identically." (line 433) — this only validates **local-filesystem** persistence across processes. It does not validate the production failure mode: pod's emptyDir wiped on restart + async git push had not fired before the crash + `load_contract_from_branch` returns None (per the docstring at contract_store.py:139-142 which is explicit: "Tries `origin/<branch>` first — the remote ref survives local cleanup and reflects the **last pushed state** — then falls back to `<branch>` in case the main repo has a local ref but no origin copy (**e.g. pre-push crashes**)"). With orchestrator-deployment.yaml's emptyDir-backed `/home` and `.egg-state` volumes, "pre-push crashes" lose the data — that is the exact case cq-3 says MUST be recoverable.

TASK-4-9's wrapper-side recovery (line 678-685) is correctly architected — read the durable state on startup — but if the underlying primitive's write semantics are best-effort-async, `egg-contract get-context` returns either nothing or stale state on the restart path. The wrapper code is correct; the storage primitive it reads from is the gap.

**Fix paths (any of these resolves the blocker; pick the one the architect's re-propose specifies):**

- **(a) Synchronous-flush variant of `save_pipeline()` / commit-and-push for HITL-decision writes + safety-budget transition writes.** A new contract-store method (e.g. `save_pipeline_sync(pipeline, fields=["no_progress_budget","parked_hitl"])`) that returns only after origin ref reflects the write. Cost: one remote-push latency per rare mutation. The plan adds a TASK-2-Nn that lands this method, and TASK-2-6 / TASK-2-7 wire to it instead of the existing best-effort `save_pipeline`.

- **(b) Different durable backing store for the safety-budget + parked-HITL state.** E.g. Redis with AOF persistence, Postgres, or a server-side blocking HTTP endpoint that fsyncs before returning. This is a larger scope change but cleanly separates HITL-critical durability from pipeline-state durability.

- **(c) Explicit operator HITL gate documenting the residual risk.** If the architect's re-propose decides the cost of (a)/(b) is too large, the plan must register a HITL decision (e.g. `mcp__sdlc__register_open_question`) asking the operator whether to accept the reduced-durability posture, AND TASK-4-11's integration test must explicitly cover the failure mode "emptyDir wipe + remote ref behind" so the residual risk is visible.

**Whichever path is chosen, TASK-2-10's host-death simulation must be REWRITTEN to test the production failure mode (not just process-exit-same-FS).** A correct simulation: writer process writes locally + emits the async push intent but the push thread does not fire → kill the writer process → wipe the emptyDir-equivalent on the test fixture (or set it up so no local state exists) → spawn a reader process → assert the reader recovers the budget from the remote ref (or fails cleanly in a way the wrapper can detect). This is the test that validates cq-3.

### Non-blocking

- **yaml-tasks slice GOAL text retains `claude -p` references at lines 238, 463, 551** even though every TASK description inside those slices uses `python3 -m egg_agent` (and TASK-4-3 actively bans `claude -p`). The slice goals are copied verbatim from the architect's slices.yaml which was already NACKed for this regression in my parallel architect-NACK. When the architect re-proposes with the primitive correction, please pull the corrected slice goals into the yaml-tasks appendix so the plan is internally consistent. Acceptance-criterion grep guards prevent code-level regression, but a coder skimming the slice goal first could still build the wrong mental model.

- **slice-3 DAG dependency.** slice-3 `depends_on: [slice-2]` but TASK-3-1's acceptance is "If `docs/spike/2908-event-pump-spike.md` is missing at implement time, the implementer must NACK upward — slice-3 cannot ship without the slice-1 decision." The effective dependency graph is slice-3 → {slice-1, slice-2}. The DAG is a tree, so slice-3 needs a single parent — but the task-level constraint adds an implicit cross-edge. Add a note to slice-3's goal "Implicit data dependency on slice-1 spike outcome via TASK-3-1's acceptance criterion; the spike report must exist before TASK-3-1 can ship." OR add an explicit `depends_on: [slice-1, slice-2]` (if the DAG model supports it). Mostly cosmetic — TASK-3-1's NACK-upward clause already enforces the dependency at implementation time.

- **R13 cross-role memory READ allowlist verification not in any task.** TASK-3-2's `_append_brc_memory_entry` is a WRITE; `shared/egg_restrictions/patterns.py:362-484` covers WRITES. But the per-event handler also READS its memory file on each invocation. Reads are checked by `tool_interceptor.check_file_write_permission`'s read-side counterpart (or are not checked because reads aren't gated). Add a one-line verification task (or fold into TASK-3-2's acceptance): confirm the agent can READ `.egg-state/agent-outputs/<role>/brc-memory.md` per its own role's permission, and document whether cross-role reads (a producer reading the reviewer's memory, or vice-versa) are intentionally blocked. Brief confirmation step, not a NACK condition on its own.

- **TASK-4-5 OVERSEER_ALERT semantics — preserved single-fire flag.** TASK-4-5's acceptance includes "(i) OVERSEER_ALERT fires exactly once (`alert_emitted` flag is honored)" which is correct but does NOT cover the host-restart case: if the alert fired on host-A and then host-A died, does host-B re-fire? The durability fix (R1) interacts here — `alert_emitted` is stored in `pipeline.no_progress_budget` per TASK-2-6, so if that field is recoverable, the re-fire is correctly suppressed; if it's not recoverable, the new host re-emits the alert, which is operator-visible noise but not a correctness regression. Worth a one-line acceptance note: "after host-restart with `alert_emitted=true` recovered from durable state, the alert is NOT re-emitted."

Verdict: NACK. Blocker is purely the inherited R1 cq-3 durability gap. The plan structurally improves on the architect's plan in several other risk-lens areas (R11 in the auth prose, R6 drift-test budget, R7 fix-lineage by name, R12 metadata-only delta, #2741 stdin/file rule, trust-boundary fixture-location call-outs), so the re-propose should be a focused durability-mechanism addition rather than a structural rewrite. The architect's re-propose path (specifying the durability mechanism) drives this — task_planner can re-propose immediately after the architect re-proposes with the storage-primitive choice.
