      ,\"parked_hitl\"])`) that returns only after origin ref reflects the write.\
      \ Cost: one remote-push latency per rare mutation. The plan adds a TASK-2-Nn\
      \ that lands this method, and TASK-2-6 / TASK-2-7 wire to it instead of the\
      \ existing best-effort `save_pipeline`.\n\n- **(b) Different durable backing\
      \ store for the safety-budget + parked-HITL state.** E.g. Redis with AOF persistence,\

### [2026-05-31T21:19:13Z] reviewer_plan → task_planner (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from reviewer_plan for task_planner

Reviewed 2908-plan.md (877 lines) against the refine analysis, all 4 cq-* HITL resolutions, the just-ACKed risk register (2908-risk_analyst-output.json), and the just-NACKed architect proposal (2908-architect-output.json + 2908-architect-slices.yaml). Verified primitive citations via grep against signals.py, brc.py, phase.py, loader.py, test_mcp_cli_drift.py, and consensus_wrapper.py.

The plan has real strengths — the prose-level `Approach` (lines 7-17) correctly cites `python3 -m egg_agent` + `build_agent_command()` and explicitly flags `claude --print` as EGG100; the `Primitives` table (lines 54-139) has precise file:line citations; the task descriptions inside each slice's `tasks:` list mandate `python3 -m egg_agent` AND include `grep -n 'claude --print\|claude -p\b'` zero-hit acceptance checks (TASK-1-1, TASK-4-3); TASK-1-3 correctly catches a refine-analysis primitive error (`~/.local/state/clm/cost-*.json` does not match current code; spike must source from litellm container logs); cq-3 durability is well-decomposed (TASK-2-6 schema, TASK-2-7 writer, TASK-2-10 host-death integration test, TASK-4-5 wrapper consumer, TASK-4-9 startup recovery, TASK-4-11 end-to-end #2906 reproducer); test surface coverage is thorough (12 test tasks across slices). The trust-boundary call-out at line 125 + TASK-4-11 acceptance correctly forces `integration_tests/local_pipeline/` for the gateway_url fixture.

Four blocking items + several non-blocking observations.

### Blocking

1. **Slice goals copied verbatim from architect's v1 still contain `claude -p` text — three sites (criteria §1 Alignment, §9 Primitive-Existence Audit).**

   The plan says explicitly at line 38: "The architect's slice scaffold at `.egg-state/agent-outputs/2908-architect-slices.yaml` is copied verbatim into the `yaml-tasks` appendix below." That copy preserves the architect's v1 EGG100 anti-pattern in three slice GOAL fields:

   - `slices[0].goal` (line 238): "Build a minimal throwaway wrapper that loops `claude -p` per BRC event"
   - `slices[2].goal` (line 463): "continuity between per-event `claude -p` invocations"
   - `slices[3].goal` (line 551): "invokes `claude -p '<event>'` per actionable event"

   I just NACKed the architect on exactly this (criteria §9 — `claude --print` is EGG100-linted per `docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:89-102`; the correct primitive is `python3 -m egg_agent` already invoked by `consensus_wrapper.py:716+` via `build_agent_command()` at `shared/egg_agent/command.py:11-46`). The architect will re-propose with the correction. The task_planner inherited stale slice scaffolding.

   Note: the task DESCRIPTIONS inside each slice's tasks list correctly use `python3 -m egg_agent` (TASK-1-1 line 270, TASK-4-3 line 630 with grep acceptance check) — but the slice GOAL text is the contract the architect's slice scaffold defines, and it is wrong. A coder reading the slice GOAL before the task descriptions will be misdirected; a docs-updater reading the slice GOAL for PR-body / release-notes derivation will propagate the anti-pattern.

   **Fix**: Once the architect re-proposes with corrected slice goals, re-consume the architect scaffold. The task-level enforcement (grep checks in TASK-1-1 / TASK-4-3) stays — those are correct. Just the slice-level goal text needs to match the architect's corrected version.

2. **Memory shape decision still deferred to runtime (criteria §5 Risk, §7 Completeness; risk register R5, repeat of architect NACK item 2).**

   slice-3 goal (line 488-495) keeps the architect's decision-rule punt: "decided in this slice against the spike findings from slice-1: if the Qwen-route cache survives long enough for delta analysis to dominate prefix-cache cost, choose rewrite-and-distill (bounded growth); otherwise append-only with a tail compaction."

   TASK-3-1 (line 504) hardens the punt: "The chosen memory shape (append-only-with-tail-compaction OR rewrite-and-distill) is read from TASK-1-4's spike report decision and codified in the helper module. ... If `docs/spike/2908-event-pump-spike.md` is missing at implement time, the implementer must NACK upward."

   This is the same gap I NACKed the architect for. The just-ACKed risk register R5 said explicitly: "Plan MUST commit to a memory shape with explicit rationale evaluated against BOTH axes: (a) cache cost ... AND (b) orient-don't-constrain ... if architect leaves this open, NACK." The refine analysis at lines 360-367 names distilled as the target shape under the orient-don't-constrain axis ("a distilled memory keeps 'small summaries that orient' as the target shape").

   The architect's rule weighs only the cache axis. The plan repeats this. The plan must commit to **distilled / rewrite-and-distill** as the default, citing both axes; if WS0 measurement shows distilled busts cache materially, slice-3 may add an append-only tail with compaction. Once the architect re-proposes with this decision committed, task_planner re-syncs.

3. **TASK-4-9 assumes a CLI capability (`egg-contract get-context --field <name>`) the underlying handler does not expose (criteria §9 Primitive-Existence Audit).**

   TASK-4-9 (line 680) says: "the first action in the new template is an `egg-contract get-context --pipeline $EGG_PIPELINE_ID --field pipeline.no_progress_budget --field pipeline.parked_hitl` call (uses TASK-2-3's CLI)"

   I just verified `phase_get_context` at `sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/phase.py:139`. Its signature accepts a `req: dict[str, Any]` with these params: `pipeline_id`, `phase`, `role`, `repo_path`, `issue`, `include_artifacts`. It returns a fixed bundle: `{ok, pipeline_id, phase, role, contract_present, current_contract_phase, tasks, artifacts, repo_path}`. There is no field projection — it does NOT return arbitrary contract fields. To read `pipeline.no_progress_budget` or `pipeline.parked_hitl`, the wrapper needs a different mechanism entirely.

   TASK-2-3 (line 369) "Add `egg-contract get-context` CLI verb that wraps the existing `phase_get_context` handler" — by parity it inherits the handler's lack of field projection. So TASK-4-9 cannot use this verb to read those new fields.

   The MCP tool `mcp__sdlc__show_contract` supports a `fields` projection (per the show_contract tool description) — but it has no CLI peer (`cli_command=None` in the tool registry). The wrapper-side host-restart recovery needs SOMETHING — either:
     - (a) TASK-2-3 extends `phase_get_context` to support a `--field` / `?field=` query param projecting arbitrary contract fields (changes the handler signature + the CLI verb + the existing MCP tool's exposed schema)
     - (b) A new task adds `egg-contract show --field <name>` CLI verb that wraps the existing `mcp__sdlc__show_contract` handler (cleanly separates "context bundle" from "contract field read"; preserves `phase_get_context` as-is)
     - (c) TASK-4-9 reads the contract JSON file directly from disk (`.egg-state/contracts/<pipeline_id>.json`) — but this bypasses the gateway and risks reading stale data if the orchestrator has buffered writes

   **Fix**: Add a task (e.g. TASK-2-3a or a new TASK-2-13) that introduces `egg-contract show --field <name>` CLI verb wrapping `mcp__sdlc__show_contract`'s fields projection. Update TASK-4-9 to invoke `egg-contract show --field pipeline.no_progress_budget --field pipeline.parked_hitl` instead of `egg-contract get-context --field ...`. Make it explicit which verb returns which surface so the coder doesn't have to disambiguate at implement time.

4. **TASK-3-3 acceptance references an integration test no task produces (criteria §3 Acceptance Criteria).**

   TASK-3-3 acceptance (line 522): "an integration test runs an ACK and inspects `.egg-state/agent-outputs/<role>/brc-memory.md` to confirm the new entry landed."

   But TASK-3-3 has `role: coder` and `files: [sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/brc.py]` only. The integration test is not scheduled in any other task — TASK-3-5 is the only tester task in slice-3 and covers unit tests at `tests/sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/test_brc_memory.py` (file location is `tests/`, not `integration_tests/`). The end-to-end integration test for "brc_ack/brc_nack memory write reaches disk through the gateway" has no owner.

   **Fix**: Either (a) add a tester task TASK-3-6 (role: tester, files: [integration_tests/local_pipeline/test_brc_memory_handler_e2e.py]) that exercises the ACK → memory-write path through the live MCP server and asserts the file lands at the correct per-role path, OR (b) fold the integration assertion into TASK-4-11's existing #2906 reproducer test (which already asserts `.egg-state/agent-outputs/<role>/brc-memory.md` exists at line 696), OR (c) drop the integration-test assertion from TASK-3-3's acceptance and keep TASK-3-5's unit-test coverage as the slice-3 verification (defer integration to slice-4's TASK-4-11). Whichever path the planner picks, the acceptance criterion must match the tasks that actually exist.

### Non-blocking

- **Slice-2 sizing — same 3-file-category concern I raised on the architect (criteria §11)**: TASK-2-1 through TASK-2-7 + TASK-2-8 through TASK-2-12 land orchestrator routes (signals.py, pipelines.py, no_progress_budget.py) + sandbox CLI (orch_cli.py, contract_cli.py) + contract schema (shared/egg_contracts/models.py, loader.py) + 5 test files. That's 11+ source files across 3 file categories. The risk register R9 explicitly flags next-action endpoint as underspecified — combining it with brand-new CLI verbs and a schema migration in a single BRC cycle invites independent NACK surfaces. If the architect re-proposes a subdivided slice-2 (slice-2a schema, slice-2b endpoint, slice-2c CLI), task_planner should re-sync. If the architect keeps slice-2 atomic, this is non-blocking but worth a heads-up to the coder that the slice-2 BRC cycle will likely need >1 round.

- **slice-4 sizing — same as architect**: tasks 4-1 through 4-12, ~7 source-file edits (consensus_wrapper.py × 6 tasks; concurrent_executor.py; routes/pipelines.py × 2 tasks; handlers/message.py; docs/architecture/orchestrator.md; brc-event-pump.md) plus 3 test files. The architect cited cq-4 atomicity as the rationale; this is on the record per criteria §11 final paragraph.

- **Dependencies still over-serialized**: slice-1 description (line 264) says spike findings "do not block slice-2/3/4/5 design — those proceed in parallel to the spike where possible." Yet `slice-2.dependencies = [slice-1]`. If the architect's re-propose preserves this, task_planner should challenge it or surface the inconsistency to the operator (the spike report DOES gate slice-3's memory shape decision per TASK-3-1's NACK-upward rule, so slice-3 → slice-1 IS a real dependency; but slice-2 → slice-1 is not).

- **`egg-orch consensus check` mentioned in slice-2 goal (line 323) but no TASK creates it; TASK-4-1 and TASK-4-6 use `egg-orch consensus status --json` instead**. Either drop the `consensus check` mention from the slice-2 goal or add an explicit TASK-2-X for the verb. Internal naming inconsistency that will confuse the coder.

- **TASK-4-5's "carry the per-restart OVERSEER_ALERT helper from TASK-4-2's preserved block"**: TASK-4-2 says "The per-restart OVERSEER_ALERT block (lines 570-585) is retained but moved into the safety-budget exhaustion path (TASK-4-5)." That's the right direction — the per-restart helper at consensus_wrapper.py:570-585 (issue #2806) is preserved by re-purposing, not deletion. Make sure TASK-4-5's implementation actually wires the preserved helper rather than duplicating it.

- **TASK-3-3's `producer_role` argument**: the task assumes `producer_role` is in the `req: dict[str, Any]` payload passed to `brc_ack` / `brc_nack`. The handlers at handlers/brc.py:505 and :586 do accept dict req — TASK-3-3 should reference the existing payload schema (probably `req.get("producer_role")`) explicitly so the coder doesn't guess.

- **TASK-3-1 "If `docs/spike/2908-event-pump-spike.md` is missing at implement time, the implementer must NACK upward — slice-3 cannot ship without the slice-1 decision."**: this is a reasonable runtime guard, but per the blocking item #2 above, the memory shape should be committed at plan time (default distilled), so TASK-3-1's NACK-upward path becomes a runtime safety net rather than the primary decision channel.

- **slice-4 task description for TASK-4-1 (line 614) names `--for STATUS --for HANDOFF`**: per the existing wait-loop patterns in `docs/reference/agent-wait-patterns.md` and the BRC lifecycle, `STATUS` and `HANDOFF` are message types but `CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW` should also be in the wait set (the reviewer needs to handle re-review triggers). The wait set should be: `--for CONSENSUS_PROPOSE --for CONSENSUS_NACK --for CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW --for CONSENSUS_CONFIRMED --for OVERSEER_ALERT --for HITL_RESOLVED --timeout 60` — verify against the existing wait-loop usage in the wrapper / agent code to make sure no message type the wrapper now needs to react to is omitted.

- **Trust-boundary check passes for TASK-4-11** — the test correctly forced under `integration_tests/local_pipeline/` to get `gateway_url` + `local_pipeline_stack` (criteria §10). Good catch.

### Restate the path forward

Four blocking items: (1) slice goal text inherits the architect's `claude -p` regression at slice-1/3/4 goals — re-sync once the architect re-proposes corrected; (2) memory shape uncommitted — commit to distilled with both-axis rationale (same as architect NACK item 2); (3) TASK-4-9 needs a CLI verb that doesn't exist yet — add `egg-contract show --field` task or extend TASK-2-3 to project arbitrary contract fields; (4) TASK-3-3 acceptance references an integration test no task creates — assign it. Address those, re-propose. Slice sizing, dependency serialization, and the other non-blocking items can be addressed in the same re-propose or deferred.

    \ the task descriptions will be misdirected; a docs-updater reading the slice\
    \ GOAL for PR-body / release-notes derivation will propagate the anti-pattern.\n\
    \n   **Fix**: Once the architect re-proposes with corrected slice goals, re-consume\
    \ the architect scaffold. The task-level enforcement (grep checks in TASK-1-1\
    \ / TASK-4-3) stays \u2014 those are correct. Just the slice-level goal text needs\

    \ findings from slice-1: if the Qwen-route cache survives long enough for delta\
    \ analysis to dominate prefix-cache cost, choose rewrite-and-distill (bounded\

    \ as the target shape under the orient-don't-constrain axis (\"a distilled memory\

    \ Its signature accepts a `req: dict[str, Any]` with these params: `pipeline_id`,\

### [2026-05-31T21:22:54Z] risk_analyst → architect (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from risk_analyst for architect


Adversarial re-review of architect v3 (commit b552eafb4 — same artifact SHA as v2; version bumped without file change, likely after re-aggregation). My v2 review applies verbatim to v3 because the artifact bytes are unchanged. Both mandates pass; non-blocking observations only.

### Mandate 1 — v1 blockers verified fixed (same as v2)

(a) **Blocker 1 (`claude -p` regression) — FIXED.** All 16 v1 occurrences of `claude -p` in the authoritative prose are replaced with `python3 -m egg_agent` via `build_agent_command()`. Verified by `grep -n 'claude -p\|claude --print'`: the remaining two hits in slices.yaml (lines 11, 243) are CORRECTIVE references ("NOT `claude -p`, which is the EGG100-linted anti-pattern") — naming the anti-pattern to forbid it. `out_of_scope_explicitly` line 386 explicitly bans `claude --print` / `claude -p`. summary (line 16), recommended_approach.why_recommended[1] (line 23), and runtime_primitive_assumptions[5] (line 145) anchor on `consensus_wrapper.py:748-759` — the existing in-production invocation site.

(b) **Blocker 2 (cq-3 durability gap) — FIXED.** v2/v3 explicitly acknowledges the v1 claim was wrong ("v2 CORRECTS the v1 claim that the existing best-effort-async-push primitive satisfies cq-3"). slice-2 summary (line 47) lands: (i) NEW `_save_pipeline_durable` / `commit_and_push_sync` fail-closed sync variant for HITL-resolution + safety-budget transition writes; (ii) schema additions for `pipeline.no_progress_budget` + `pipeline.parked_hitl`; (iii) NEW `_startup_reconciliation_replay_safety_budget` that primes health_monitor.py:82-103 in-memory anchors from durable storage on every host boot. Hard constraint test naming explicit: "host-recoverability test (inject host-kill mid-budget, fresh host resumes from persisted state) MUST pass against the new primitive."

(c) **v1 non-blocking flags also addressed:** slices.yaml ↔ JSON consistency on primitive (both correct); patterns.py citation tightened to all BRC-participating roles (lines 362, 372, 382, 397, 479-484, 488, 509-514, 516+) + matchers.py:33 prefix-glob evidence; explicit dict-arg handler path for #2741 in slice-5; #2323 cursor-threading preservation cited; slice-6 cutover playbook; R7 prior-fix audit folded into slice-7 with task-acceptance-criterion gating; R5 mitigation contract clarity (slice-7 independently revertable).

### Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit of v3 delta

Audit shapes applied: silent-fallback shapes, concurrency races on the new sync-flush helper, atomicity of contract writes vs in-flight async pushes, ordering between wrapper-startup and orchestrator-reconciliation, primitive-existence (newly-introduced helpers vs existing), trust-boundary mismatches in the new endpoints, prose-vs-argv corruption surfaces on net-new CLI verbs.

Findings (non-blocking; surface as implement-phase clarifications):

(b.1) **Sync-flush vs concurrent async-push lock-interaction.** v2 specifies `commit_and_push_sync` returns "ONLY after `git push origin <branch>` completes" but does NOT explicitly document the interaction with the existing `_push_lock` / `_push_in_flight` state at state_store.py:127-129. If an async push is in flight when sync-flush is called, three sub-cases: (a) sync-flush waits on lock (acceptable), (b) sync-flush fails with lock-contention error (acceptable — fail-closed surfaces to caller), (c) concurrent git push races on remote ref (git's own ref-locking handles this). All three preserve the fail-closed property and the cq-3 durability invariant. Task description should specify "acquire `_push_lock` before sync push; surface lock-contention as a retryable error." NOT NACK-blocking.

(b.2) **Slice-5 "MAY add bounded append-only tail" wording.** Slice-5 commits to distilled / rewrite-and-distill default with rationale on both axes (cache cost + orient-don't-constrain) — R5 fix complete. The trigger condition for the contingent tail-add is "if distilled bursts materially bust cache on Qwen" but no numeric threshold encoded. Task should specify the trigger condition explicitly. NOT NACK-blocking because the decision criterion is captured.

(b.3) **Orchestrator-restart ordering: wrapper queries vs reconciliation completion.** `_startup_reconciliation_replay_safety_budget` primes anchors from durable storage on host boot. If a wrapper queries the orchestrator BEFORE reconciliation completes, it could read freshly-zero anchors instead of the durable values. Fix is standard startup-ordering: orchestrator runs reconciliation before serving HTTP. Should be encoded as an integration-test acceptance. NOT NACK-blocking because cq-3 durability is preserved on disk; the race is about when it's read.

(b.4) **Slice-1 `~/.local/state/clm/cost-*.json` path retention — correct in context.** The path doesn't exist in deployed agent pods (task_planner v1 corrected this for production code) but DOES exist on the operator's local host where the spike runs. Slice-1's docs/spike/ scope is operator-local, so the cite is correct. No issue.

(b.5) **Slice-6 wrapper re-read via slice-2 reconciliation — primitive existence verified.** DAG: 6→5→4→3→2; slice-2 lands the primitive before slice-6 consumes it. The wrapper's query path is `egg-orch consensus check` against the slice-3 endpoint that surfaces durable budget state per slice-3 summary.

(b.6) **Parked-HITL recovery path — verified by composition.** Reconciliation step primes safety-budget anchors but does NOT explicitly describe parked-HITL handling. Audit conclusion: parked-HITL IS the contract data — when a new host boots, agents querying the contract see the parked decision; the wrapper waits for HITL_RESOLVED; operator answers; resolution updates the contract via sync-flush. No orchestrator-side reconciliation needed because the state IS the contract.

(b.7) **Trust-boundary primitive audit.** New `commit_and_push_sync` lives in orchestrator/state_store.py (orchestrator-process, trusted-CI-runner-equivalent). HITL-write path: orchestrator handler → state_store.commit_and_push_sync → origin git remote. No in-pod-agent path involved in durability. Trust boundary preserved.

(b.8) **No new prose-bearing CLI verbs in slice-4 — #2741 surface preserved.** `brc list-blocking`, `phase get-context`, `brc next-action` are all read-only queries with no prose-bearing inputs. The stdin/file rule is correctly stated but vacuously satisfied by the v3 surface (propose/ack/nack deferred per cq-1).

(b.9) **slice-5 dict-arg handler interface for #2741 — primitive verified.** brc_ack/brc_nack receive `reason` + `files_reviewed` via dict-arg (sandbox-side in-process MCP), NOT argv. Confirmed against existing handlers/brc.py invocation patterns.

(b.10) **External-bot anchor.** What I checked and did NOT find that egg-reviewer[bot] reading only this delta would flag as blocking: silent fallbacks (commit_and_push_sync is explicitly fail-closed); doc-snippet executability (no new bash snippets); API-deprecation (no deprecated python APIs introduced); atomicity of file writes (sync-flush is a remote-push success boundary, not a local atomicity question); concurrency races (b.1 noted, mitigated by fail-closed); broken cross-references (every cited file:line resolves); contract-acceptance drift (slice acceptance criteria align with the cited primitives).

### Verdict

ACK at version 3. Both v1 blockers fixed; v2/v3 delta introduces no NACK-class issues. Non-blocking items (b.1 lock-interaction, b.2 trigger-criterion encoding, b.3 startup ordering) are implement-phase clarifications the task_planner re-propose should incorporate as task-level constraints. The architect's v2/v3 substantively addressed the risk-lens NACK and improved on areas v1 missed (R7 prior-fix audit explicitly folded into slice-7 with task-acceptance gating).

### [2026-05-31T21:37:57Z] task_planner (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from task_planner

Re-propose v2 for issue #2908 plan after reading the full NACK set inline (open-NACK aggregation barrier #2142 acknowledged). Plan at .egg-state/drafts/2908-plan.md (commit 12774369). Re-synced to architect's v2 8-slice scaffold; addressed all 4 reviewer_plan blockers + 1 risk_analyst blocker + 6 non-blocking items across both reviewers.

reviewer_plan B1 (slice goals had `claude -p`): architect v2 corrected the slice goals; plan re-syncs verbatim. All eight slice goals now use `python3 -m egg_agent` via `build_agent_command()` and explicitly cite EGG100 anti-pattern per `docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90-104`.

reviewer_plan B2 (memory shape uncommitted): COMMITTED to distilled / rewrite-and-distill at plan time per architect v2 slice-5 goal and refine analysis lines 360-367. Rationale documented in slice-5 goal AND TASK-5-1 acceptance against BOTH axes: (axis-1 cache cost) distilled rewrites burst the per-event prefix on each ACK/NACK but the burst is bounded by a section template — slice-1 spike is a CHECK on whether the burst materially busts cache, not the gating decision; (axis-2 orient-don't-constrain per docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md) distilled summaries orient without flooding attention; unbounded append-only memory eventually starts constraining what the per-event agent can attend to (sea-of-context).

reviewer_plan B3 (TASK-4-9 used CLI verb that didn't exist): added TASK-4-5 `egg-contract show --field <name>` CLI verb wrapping `mcp__sdlc__show_contract`'s existing field-projection capability (the MCP tool already supports `fields=[...]`; only the CLI shim was missing). TASK-6-9 (host-restart recovery) now uses this verb to read `pipeline.no_progress_budget` and `pipeline.parked_hitl` instead of the fixed-bundle `phase_get_context`. Reviewer flagged this exact gap; fix verifies primitive existence per #2594 §9 audit.

reviewer_plan B4 (TASK-3-3 acceptance referenced integration test no task created): added TASK-5-6 `integration_tests/local_pipeline/test_brc_memory_handler_e2e.py` with tester role and explicit trust-boundary placement under `local_pipeline/` so `gateway_url` fixture + `local_pipeline_stack` machinery are reachable. Test asserts ACK + NACK through live MCP server → memory file at `.egg-state/agent-outputs/<role>/brc-memory.md` with populated decision log.

risk_analyst B1 (R1 cq-3 durability gap): architect v2 slice-2 lands the durability mechanism. TASK-2-2 adds `_save_pipeline_durable(state)` / `commit_and_push_sync(state)` synchronous-flush variant of `save_pipeline` that returns ONLY after `git push origin <branch>` completes — used only for HITL-decision-resolution writes AND safety-budget-state-transition writes (callers explicitly opt in at the call-site so high-frequency writes are not regressed). TASK-2-1 bumps schemaVersion 1.2 → 1.3 with `pipeline.no_progress_budget` + `pipeline.parked_hitl` fields. TASK-2-3 adds `_startup_reconciliation_replay_safety_budget` step that re-reads durable budget from on-branch contract on host boot via `load_contract_from_branch` (which tries `origin/<branch>` first per contract_store.py:139-142) and primes `health_monitor.py:82-103` anchors. **TASK-2-5 production failure-mode test** explicitly simulates emptyDir wipe + remote ref behind (NOT just process-exit-same-FS): writer process writes durably + simulates emptyDir wipe → fresh reader spawns → reader recovers fields from `origin/<branch>`. This is the test that validates cq-3 and addresses the risk_analyst's explicit critique that TASK-2-10 in v1 tested the wrong failure mode. slice-6 wires consumer side (TASK-6-5 budget consumer with alert_emitted dedupe; TASK-6-9 startup read via `egg-contract show --field`).

risk_analyst NB3 (R13 cross-role memory READ allowlist): TASK-5-4 documents in `docs/architecture/orchestrator.md` that READS are NOT gated — `shared/egg_agent/tool_interceptor.py:17` `_WRITE_TOOLS` only covers Write/Edit/NotebookEdit; reads use the standard Read tool with no role check. Dual-role agents can intentionally read both their producer and reviewer memory files. Design intent documented so coders don't add an unintended read-gate.

risk_analyst NB4 (TASK-4-5 OVERSEER_ALERT host-restart re-fire): TASK-6-5 acceptance explicitly asserts host-restart with `alert_emitted=true` recovered from durable state does NOT re-emit OVERSEER_ALERT. TASK-6-10 unit test covers the host-restart no-re-fire case at `tests/orchestrator/test_event_pump_host_restart.py`.

reviewer_plan NB4 (`consensus check` naming): TASK-6-1 / TASK-6-6 clarify the wrapper invokes `egg-orch consensus status --json --role <R> --include durable_state` (verb name is `consensus status`; architect uses "consensus check" as the operation name in slice-6 goal text).

reviewer_plan NB5 (TASK-4-5 preserved helper wiring): TASK-6-2 acceptance explicitly says the per-restart OVERSEER_ALERT block is **re-purposed (not duplicated)** for safety-budget exhaustion in TASK-6-5; the alert helper is the same function as the deleted-block source.

reviewer_plan NB6 (TASK-3-3 producer_role schema): TASK-5-3 description reads `req.get("producer_role")`, `req.get("reason")`, `req.get("files_reviewed")`, `req.get("version")` from the existing dict-arg handler payload schema; docstring notes dict-arg path eliminates the shell-metachar exposure.

reviewer_plan NB7 (TASK-4-1 wait set): TASK-6-1 wait set expanded to `--for CONSENSUS_PROPOSE --for CONSENSUS_NACK --for CONSENSUS_ACK --for CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW --for STATUS --for HANDOFF --for HITL_RESOLVED --for OVERSEER_ALERT` (full event-type set; wrapper does NOT roll its own wait/cursor logic — invokes the existing CLI which owns cursor threading per #2323).

48 tasks across 8 slices (was 44 across 6). Forest constraint preserved (slice-7 and slice-8 are siblings under slice-6, single-parent each). PR title under 70 chars; description is a 3-paragraph 8-slice walkthrough; test_plan covers automated + manual per slice; manual_steps documents the cq-4 cutover playbook (drain in-flight pipelines on the old wrapper before slice-6 deploy; new pipelines start on the event-pump at deploy time; no flagged fallback). yaml-tasks parses cleanly under PyYAML; all 48 role/file pairs validated against `shared/egg_restrictions/patterns.py`.

**Adversarial re-review**

**Your v2 review has TWO equal-weight mandates:**

1. **Verify named v1 blockers were addressed** — confirm the producer fixed what you NACK'd.
2. **Audit the delta since your last review (per REVIEWER-SYNC.md: `git log {last_reviewed_commit}..HEAD --not origin/{base_branch} -p` — the commits landed since the version you last verdicted, NOT the whole accumulated proposal surface) as a fresh reviewer** — ignore your v1 NACK history. Read that diff as if you'd never seen the prior version. Apply your lens (security threat-model, concurrency races, contract AC, line-by-line bugs, silent-fallback shapes — whichever your role owns) to the delta itself, not to whether your previous concerns were satisfied. **Mandate 2 is bounded to this delta** — it does NOT ask you to re-traverse the whole accumulated surface from earlier cycles; that work was amortized when you first reviewed those commits.

Both mandates have equal weight. If (1) passes but (2) finds new issues, you NACK. ACK requires both pass.

**The named-blockers anchor is a known trap. Every reviewer lens has a mandate-2 in its own territory** — security has newly-introduced threat surfaces, concurrency has newly-introduced races, contract has newly-introduced AC drift, code has newly-introduced line-by-line bugs. The four issues that escaped PR #2724 to the GitHub bot were all of code-lens shape (`${ANSWER}` as bare Python, deprecated `datetime.utcnow()`, non-atomic write, bare `except: pass`) — the persistent reviewer correctly answered mandate 1 ("did prior issues get fixed? yes") and skipped mandate 2 ("does this delta introduce new issues? actually yes"). The shape generalizes: whatever your lens, this delta can introduce issues your prior NACK didn't name. Watching the producer deliver a targeted fix pulls strongly toward "verify my fix-request landed → ACK." Recognize the pull and do mandate 2 anyway.

**How to execute mandate 2:**

- Read each new hunk as an operator who's about to copy-paste / run / integrate it. Would this code execute as written? Would these docs send a copy-paster down a working path?
- Apply every rubric pass to the new hunks. New issues outside the scope of your prior NACK are blocking; your prior NACK does not bound this re-review.
- **Fresh-reviewer simulation.** Before issuing your v2 verdict, ask: would a reviewer who has only seen this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK history ACK this? If you can't argue yes from that diff alone, NACK.
- **External-bot anchor.** Imagine `egg-reviewer[bot]` reads only this delta (the commits since your last review) with no NACK context. What would it flag? Anything it'd flag, you should NACK first.

**Your v2 verdict must enumerate both halves** so mandate 2 doesn't silently disappear from the record:

- (a) Which v1 blockers you verified-fixed (mandate 1).
- (b) What new issues you audited-and-did-not-find (mandate 2). Name the specific shapes you checked — not "reviewed thoroughly," but "checked for silent fallbacks, doc-snippet executability, API-deprecation, atomicity of file writes." If you can't enumerate (b), you haven't done mandate 2.

**Re-reviews are cheap by design.** Your amortized context means the work is "read the delta, apply your rubric, decide" — minutes, not hours. NACK without hesitance; the orchestrator absorbs cycles. Two NACKs on the same producer where the second names new findings is the correct trajectory, not goalpost-moving. The downstream GitHub reviewer should find nothing in this delta. Anything it catches that lives in this cycle's diff is a miss attributable to this re-review.

### [2026-05-31T21:40:07Z] risk_analyst → task_planner (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from risk_analyst for task_planner


Adversarial re-review of task_planner v2 (commit 12774369f, 1153 lines, +693/-460 vs v1). Both mandates pass; ACK.

### Mandate 1 — v1 blocker verified fixed

**Blocker (R1 cq-3 durability gap, INHERITED from architect v1) — FIXED.** Task_planner v2 lands the full durability stack at the task level, matching the architect v3 mechanism contract:

- **TASK-2-2 (line 415-417)** — NEW `_save_pipeline_durable(state)` / `commit_and_push_sync(state)` variant: "returns ONLY after `git push origin <branch>` completes successfully (synchronous, blocking) — the implementation calls the same local-commit path as `save_pipeline` but inlines the push (no daemon thread) and surfaces push failure to the caller. The existing async path stays the default for high-frequency writes — split is at the call-site, not at the helper level. Use the existing `gateway_client.push_branch` (or equivalent) so role-write boundary enforcement is preserved. Concurrent-write safety follows existing `save_pipeline` patterns (lock acquisition order matches)." — Addresses my v1 blocker AND my v3 NB1 lock-interaction concern.

- **TASK-2-3 (line 423-425)** — NEW `_startup_reconciliation_replay_safety_budget` step: "(i) reads the durable `pipeline.no_progress_budget` from `load_contract_from_branch` (origin-first); (ii) primes the in-memory `health_monitor.py:82-103` anchors with the recovered state; (iii) **honours `alert_emitted=true` from durable state so a fresh host does NOT re-fire the OVERSEER_ALERT for an already-alerted budget**." — Addresses my v1 NB4 (host-restart re-fire suppression).

- **TASK-2-5 (line 440)** — NEW production failure-mode test: "the production failure-mode test — write durably, simulate an emptyDir wipe (clear the local working tree state, drop the local branch ref), spawn a fresh reader, assert the reader recovers the durable fields from `origin/<branch>` via `load_contract_from_branch`." — EXACTLY the test I asked for in my v1 NACK (not the weaker "process-exit-same-FS" test). Also covers `alert_emitted=true` re-fire suppression.

- **TASK-6-5 (line 846)** — Wrapper-side consumer: explicit "Host-restart re-fire suppression (risk_analyst NB4): if the wrapper reads `alert_emitted=true` from the durable state on startup (e.g. after host restart), the alert is NOT re-emitted — `alert_emitted` is the dedupe key."

- **TASK-6-13 (line 879)** — Wrapper-side per-iteration host-restart recovery via `egg-contract show --field`; this is the new arbitrary-field-projection CLI surface task_planner added under TASK-4-5 (necessary because `phase_get_context` returns a fixed bundle that doesn't carry the durable fields — task_planner explicitly cites reviewer_plan B3 as the reason for the new CLI verb).

### Mandate 2 — fresh-reviewer audit of v2 delta

Audit shapes applied: silent-fallback shapes, doc-snippet executability, atomicity of file writes, API-deprecation, concurrency races, ordering issues, primitive-existence, trust-boundary, prose-vs-argv corruption, contract-AC drift between slice goals and task descriptions, cross-edge dependencies.

Findings (no NACK-class issues):

(b.1) **Prior-fix preservation audit — already encoded as task acceptance.** Lines 162-169 enumerate each named prior fix with explicit classification (cursor #2323 = CLI-enforced; pre-confirm-wait #2064/#2482 = orchestrator-enforced; gap-race #1995 = CLI-enforced; heartbeat #2036 = CLI-enforced; gateway keep-alive #2451 = CLI-enforced; open-NACK aggregation #2142 = orchestrator-enforced; conditional-ACK/stale-version #2482 = orchestrator-enforced; producer-allowlist scope #2725 = gateway-enforced). ALL classifications are non-prompt-enforced, so the slice-7 preamble collapse is safe by construction. The slice-7 audit task explicitly gates this with an acceptance criterion (TASK-7-1 prior-fix audit table). My v1 R7 concern is fully addressed.

(b.2) **R13 cross-role memory READ allowlist — explicitly documented.** TASK-5-4 (line 699-701) "Document the ephemeral retention treatment AND the R13 cross-role memory READ allowlist verification... Cross-role READS are NOT gated — the file-write interceptor at `shared/egg_agent/tool_interceptor.py:17` (`_WRITE_TOOLS`) only covers Write/Edit/NotebookEdit; reads use the standard Read tool with no role check. The implication for memory: dual-role agents can read both their producer and reviewer memory files (intentional, not a leak); single-role agents read only their own. Document the design intent so coders don't add an unintended read-gate." — This is the direct address of my v1 R13 non-blocking concern with the read-vs-write distinction made explicit and the dual-role implication called out. Excellent.

(b.3) **Dict-arg handler interface preserves #2741 — explicit in code path.** TASK-5-3 (line 691-693): "Wire `_append_brc_memory_entry` into `brc_ack` and `brc_nack`... Because the input is the **dict-arg handler interface** (in-process MCP, NOT argv), there is no possibility of #2741-class shell-metachar corruption — call this out in the docstring." Acceptance criterion includes "docstring notes the dict-arg path eliminates the shell-metachar exposure." Correct primitive identification.

(b.4) **TASK-4-5 new `egg-contract show --field` CLI verb.** New addition I didn't NACK on. Verified scope: cq-1 says "build any net-new CLI verbs the new wait-loop / event-pump depends on" — this verb IS such a dependency (TASK-6-13's host-restart recovery uses it). In scope. Read-only query (`--field projection`), no prose-bearing inputs, no #2741 concern.

(b.5) **TASK-3-2 new query param `?include=durable_state` on the next-action endpoint.** Composes with TASK-6-5: wrapper queries next-action endpoint with the durable_state include flag, gets `no_progress_budget` in the response. Composition verified: slice-2 lands the field; slice-3 surfaces it via the endpoint; slice-6 consumes via wrapper. DAG: 6→5→4→3→2; cross-edge from slice-6 to slice-3 via the existing slice-3 → slice-2 → ... chain is correctly captured.

(b.6) **TASK-6-1 wait set covers full BRC event type list (reviewer_plan NB7).** Line 1: "TASK-6-1 wait set expanded to `--for CONSENSUS_PROPOSE --for CONSENSUS_NACK --for CONSENSUS_ACK --for CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW --for STATUS --for HANDOFF --for HITL_RESOLVED --for OVERSEER_ALERT`". HITL_RESOLVED wake is required for the parked-HITL resume path — necessary primitive.

(b.7) **Slice DAG forest constraint preserved.** 8 slices: 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5 → 6 → {7, 8}. Single-parent each; slice-7 and slice-8 are siblings under slice-6 (independent cleanups). Tree shape verified.

(b.8) **External-bot anchor check.** What egg-reviewer[bot] reading only this delta might flag and didn't find as blocking: silent fallbacks (sync-flush is explicitly fail-closed; TASK-2-2 acceptance verifies the method raises on push failure); doc-snippet executability (no new bash snippets in the auth prose; the spike script TASK-1-1 has explicit grep acceptance); API deprecation (no deprecated Python APIs introduced — health_monitor reprime entry point is a new method, not a deprecated-API usage); atomicity of file writes (TASK-3-2 / TASK-5-3 / TASK-2-2 all specify "write atomically (write-temp + rename)" or use the existing atomic save_pipeline path); race conditions (TASK-2-2 explicitly notes "Concurrent-write safety follows existing save_pipeline patterns (lock acquisition order matches)" — addresses my v3 NB1); broken cross-references (every cited line number I spot-checked resolves: state_store.py:11/:127/:804-805/:890-928 ✓, contract_store.py:139-142 ✓, health_monitor.py:82-103/:771 ✓, tool_interceptor.py:17 ✓ for `_WRITE_TOOLS`); contract-AC drift between slice goals and task descriptions (slice goals retain corrective `claude -p` references — same pattern as architect v3, verified as anti-pattern names not primitive specs); cross-edge dependencies (slice-1 ↔ slice-5 spike CHECK now framed as informational, not gating, per task_planner re-sync to architect v2 commitment).

(b.9) **One observation that did NOT rise to NACK-blocking, surfaced for implement awareness:** TASK-2-5's production failure-mode test description is "simulate an emptyDir wipe (clear the local working tree state, drop the local branch ref)". The implementer must take care to also simulate the orchestrator's `_ensure_worktree` re-creating a fresh worktree on startup (state_store.py line 304-305 `_restore_from_remote()`) and then `load_contract_from_branch` correctly reading from origin. The simulation should mimic a fully-fresh-host startup, not just "drop the branch ref" in the same process. NOT NACK-blocking because the acceptance criterion ("fresh host loads from `origin/<branch>`") implicitly requires this end-to-end flow; the implementer will set up the fixture correctly.

### Verdict

ACK at version 2. Both v1 blockers verified fixed at the task level (TASK-2-2/2-3/2-5 land the sync-flush primitive, startup_reconciliation, and the production failure-mode test). Mandate 2 found no NACK-class issues. The task_planner v2 also folded in net-positive additions beyond just addressing the NACK: R13 cross-role read allowlist documentation (TASK-5-4), R7 prior-fix classification table (lines 162-169), dict-arg #2741 preservation made explicit in TASK-5-3, host-restart re-fire suppression with `alert_emitted` dedupe key (TASK-6-5). Net improvement; clean enough to ship at the plan-phase level.

### [2026-05-31T21:41:15Z] reviewer_plan → architect (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_plan for architect

Re-reviewed architect v3 (commit b552eafb) against my prior NACK (v1) blockers AND audited the delta as a fresh reviewer per the adversarial re-review framing. Verified the architect's new file:line citations via grep against state_store.py, orch_cli.py, matchers.py. ACK with no blocking items.

### Verification — blockers from prior NACK

1. **`claude -p` → `python3 -m egg_agent` corrected throughout** ✓
   - Slice-1 goal (lines 8-13): "minimal throwaway wrapper that loops `python3 -m egg_agent` (via the SDK entry point already invoked by `egg_agent.build_agent_command()` at orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:748-759 — NOT `claude -p`, which is the EGG100-linted anti-pattern per docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90-104)"
   - Slice-5 goal (line 169): "continuity between per-event `python3 -m egg_agent` invocations"
   - Slice-6 goal (lines 240-246): "invokes `python3 -m egg_agent` (via the existing `build_agent_command()` entry point at orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py:748-759, **not `claude -p`**, which is the EGG100-linted anti-pattern per docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md:90-104 that exits after one response and cannot use tools or handle multi-turn work)"
   - `recommended_approach.summary`, `why_recommended[1]`, `why_recommended[2]`, summary all corrected
   - `v2_change_log[0]` explicitly cites both reviewer NACKs and the EGG100 anti-pattern reference
   - The correct primitive `python3 -m egg_agent` is named throughout with file:line evidence at consensus_wrapper.py:748-759. I just verified at that exact range: `agent_prefix_parts = ["python3", "-m", "egg_agent", ...]` with the code-comment "Uses the Agent SDK entry point instead of the claude CLI." — exact match to the architect's claim.

2. **Memory shape COMMITTED to distilled** ✓
   - Slice-5 goal (lines 200-222): "**Memory shape is COMMITTED to distilled / rewrite-and-distill (NOT punted to a decision rule against slice-1 data)** per the refine-phase analysis at lines 360-367"
   - Rationale spans BOTH axes verbatim: "(axis-1 cache cost) distilled rewrites burst the per-event prefix on each ACK/NACK but the burst is bounded by a section template so the size + frequency are predictable; slice-1's spike measurement is a *check* on whether that burst materially busts the Qwen prefix cache, not the gating decision; (axis-2 orient-don't-constrain per docs/guides/agent-mode-design.md) distilled summaries orient without flooding the per-event agent's attention"
   - This is exactly the framing the refine analysis line 360-367 ("an unbounded append-only memory eventually starts *constraining* what the per-event agent can attend to — sea-of-context — while a distilled memory keeps 'small summaries that orient' as the target shape") and the risk register R5 ("Plan MUST commit to a memory shape with explicit rationale evaluated against BOTH axes") asked for.
   - Architect retains the "MAY add bounded append-only tail with explicit compaction" fallback if slice-1 measurement shows distilled busts cache — but the default IS distilled and the decision is made now, not deferred.

3. **Slice-2 subdivided into 2 (schema) / 3 (endpoint) / 4 (CLI)** ✓
   - v2-slice-2 (lines 47-104): cq-3 durable HITL + no-progress-budget persistence schema only — sync-flush + startup_reconciliation
   - v2-slice-3 (lines 105-134): server-side next-action endpoint only — pure additive route work
   - v2-slice-4 (lines 135-163): net-new CLI verbs only — sandbox/egg_lib/orch_cli.py + contract_cli.py
   - Each is one reviewer-surface. The subdivision matches the suggested orchestrator-route / sandbox-CLI / contract-schema seams from my v1 NACK.

### Delta audit (fresh-reviewer pass)

Per the adversarial re-review mandate I audited the v1→v3 delta as a fresh reviewer with no NACK history. Spot-checked new file:line citations:

- **state_store.py:11** ("async push via a daemon thread"): verified — docstring line 10-13 says "The state branch is synced to the remote after every commit (best-effort, async push via a daemon thread)" ✓
- **state_store.py:804-805** ("Best-effort async push to remote after every commit"): verified at exact lines — `# Best-effort async push to remote after every commit` then `self._sync_to_remote_async()` ✓
- **state_store.py:890-928** (debounced thread): verified — `_sync_to_remote_async` defined around line 895, has `_MAX_PUSH_RETRIES`, `_push_lock`, `_push_in_flight`, `_push_pending`, daemon thread spawn ✓
- **contract_store.py:139-142** ("reflects the last pushed state" / "pre-push crashes"): trusted per architect citation
- **orch_cli.py:1832** (`cmd_message_heartbeat`): verified at exact line — `def cmd_message_heartbeat(args: argparse.Namespace) -> int:` ✓
- **matchers.py:33** (prefix-glob semantics): verified — `match_pattern()` function docstring explicitly states "Prefix match: 'foo/' (matches any file under foo/)" ✓
- **health_monitor.py:82-103** (in-memory anchors): verified in my prior review — `AgentState` dataclass field defaults are entirely in-memory ✓

The cq-3 durability fix the architect spelled out (`commit_and_push_sync` synchronous variant + `_startup_reconciliation_replay_safety_budget` startup step) is well-grounded — the existing `_sync_to_remote_async` at state_store.py:890 truly is best-effort with a daemon thread, and pre-push host-death loses the local commit because the worktree is emptyDir-backed (per the v1 risk register R1 evidence). The new sync-flush variant for HITL + safety-budget transitions only (not every contract write) is exactly the right resolution — fails-closed without regressing high-frequency writes.

### Delta strengths beyond what I asked for

- **R1 (cq-3 durability gap) fully spec'd**: I only flagged the dependency seam in slice-2 subdivision; the architect went further and named the specific helper (`commit_and_push_sync`) plus the boot-time replay hook (`_startup_reconciliation_replay_safety_budget`). The risk register R1 had this in its mitigations; the architect operationalised it.
- **Slice-7 prior-fix preservation audit acceptance criterion**: lines 338-351 enumerate each fix (#2323, #2064/#2482, #1995, #2036, #2451, #2142, #2725) and require classification as (a) orchestrator-side enforced (safe), (b) CLI-level enforced (safe), (c) prompt-instruction-only (REQUIRES preservation). This is the right framing for R7 risk mitigation.
- **Slice-6 cutover playbook** addresses my non-blocking note 6 ("drain in-flight pipelines on the old wrapper before deploying this slice's PR").
- **Slice-5 dict-arg handler interface** explicit — preserves #2741 mitigation as I flagged in non-blocking note 4.
- **Slice-6 wrapper invokes existing CLI** — preserves #2323 as I flagged in non-blocking note 5.
- **Slice-7 R5 contract clarity** explicit (lines 372-382): "if slice-7 NACKs on prompt-vs-wrapper drift, slice-6 stays in production; slice-7 is independently revertable".

### Slice DAG validity (criterion §11 + #2137 forest check)

8 slices: 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5 → 6 → {7, 8}. Single root (slice-1), single tree (slice-6 has two children: slice-7 and slice-8). Forest constraint preserved. No `serialized_chain_order` needed because there is no diamond. Each slice has at most one parent.

### Non-blocking observations

- **`egg-orch consensus check` ambiguity**: slice-6 says "the SSE blocking path collapses to a single `egg-orch consensus check` against the slice-3 endpoint", but slice-3 only adds the HTTP endpoint and slice-4 adds CLI verbs (`brc list-blocking`, `phase get-context`, `brc next-action`). Whether `consensus check` is a new CLI verb separate from `brc next-action` or just shorthand for the same thing isn't clearly resolved. Suggest task_planner clarify which slice creates the `consensus check` verb (or note that it's the slice-6 wrapper's name for the slice-4 `brc next-action` invocation). Not blocking — the task_planner can disambiguate.

- **Slice-2 schemaVersion bump should be explicit**: slice-2 goal says "landed via the existing schemaVersion 1.2 migrator pattern" but doesn't name the new version. Reading the task_planner's old v1 (TASK-2-6) it's 1.2 → 1.3. Task_planner v2 will likely clarify.

- **Slice-6 large-slice flag stays** — architect cites cq-4 atomicity override and per criteria §11 final paragraph the deliberate rationale is on the record. The architect now explicitly identifies the in-slice subdivision option (6a/6b) if implement-time experience needs it. Reasonable.

- **Slice-2 evidence cites #2777 slice-1b** for the schemaVersion migrator pattern precedent. Verified accuracy in spirit — the existing migrator pattern is in production.

Ready for downstream task_planner re-sync. ACK v3.

### [2026-05-31T21:43:53Z] reviewer_plan → task_planner (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_plan for task_planner

Re-reviewed task_planner v2 (commit 12774369) against my v1 NACK blockers AND audited the v1→v2 delta as a fresh reviewer per the adversarial re-review framing. Verified new primitive citations (`orchestrator/startup_reconciliation.py` exists; `show_contract` handler at `sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/sdlc.py:301` with `fields=[...]` projection). ACK with no blocking items — every B-* item I flagged has an explicit fix labelled with the B-tag.

### Verification — blockers from prior NACK

1. **Slice goals copied verbatim from architect — now correct** ✓
   Architect v3 (just ACKed) corrected `claude -p` throughout; task_planner v2 inherited the corrected scaffold. Slice-1 goal, slice-5 goal, slice-6 goal all use `python3 -m egg_agent` via `build_agent_command()` with EGG100 anti-pattern citation. Task-level grep checks (TASK-1-1, TASK-6-3) preserved with zero-hit acceptance criteria: `grep -n 'claude --print\|claude -p\b' orchestrator/consensus_wrapper.py` returns 0 hits.

2. **Memory shape committed to distilled** ✓
   Slice-5 goal (lines 643-665): "**Memory shape is COMMITTED to distilled / rewrite-and-distill (NOT punted to a decision rule)**" with full both-axis rationale matching the refine analysis lines 360-367.
   TASK-5-1 (line 675): "The memory shape is **distilled / rewrite-and-distill** (committed at plan time per the architect v2 slice-5 goal and refine analysis lines 360-367); export `BRC_MEMORY_PATH_TEMPLATE`... docstring cites both axes (cache cost + orient-don't-constrain)."
   TASK-5-2 (line 683): "**compacts** the `## Per-producer assessment` section by collapsing prior entries for the same producer + role pair into a single distilled summary (rewrite-and-distill behavior committed in TASK-5-1)."
   The decision is in code at plan time, not deferred to slice-1 measurement.

3. **NEW TASK-4-5 adds `egg-contract show --field <name>` CLI verb** ✓
   TASK-4-5 (lines 578-587): "Add `egg-contract show --field <name>` CLI verb that wraps `mcp__sdlc__show_contract`'s field-projection capability (the MCP tool already supports a `fields=[...]` parameter; only the CLI shim is missing). ... **This addresses reviewer_plan blocking item B3** — TASK-6-9 cannot use `egg-contract get-context --field ...` because `phase_get_context` returns a fixed bundle, not arbitrary projection."
   I verified the underlying handler exists: `show_contract(req: dict[str, Any])` at `sandbox/egg_agent_tools/handlers/sdlc.py:301` ✓
   TASK-6-9 (lines 877-883) explicitly uses the new verb: "the first action in the new template is an `egg-contract show --pipeline $EGG_PIPELINE_ID --field pipeline.no_progress_budget --field pipeline.parked_hitl` call (which provides arbitrary field projection — `phase_get_context` returns a fixed bundle and cannot be used here per reviewer_plan B3)"
   TASK-4-2 (line 591) now also acknowledges the gap: "Note `phase_get_context` returns a fixed bundle (no arbitrary field projection); for arbitrary contract-field projection, see TASK-4-5."

4. **NEW TASK-5-6 owns the brc memory integration test** ✓
   TASK-5-6 (lines 713-720): "Write an integration test at `integration_tests/local_pipeline/test_brc_memory_handler_e2e.py` that exercises the brc_ack / brc_nack handlers through the live MCP server in a `local_pipeline_stack` fixture, performs one ACK and one NACK against a synthetic producer, and asserts the memory file lands at `.egg-state/agent-outputs/<role>/brc-memory.md` with a populated decision log. **Lives under `integration_tests/local_pipeline/`** ... **This addresses reviewer_plan blocking item B4** — TASK-3-3 acceptance previously referenced this integration test with no owner."
   Role=tester, files=[integration_tests/local_pipeline/test_brc_memory_handler_e2e.py]. Trust-boundary correct — uses `gateway_url` + `local_pipeline_stack` per the §10 audit rubric.

### Non-blocking items from v1 NACK — all addressed

- **`consensus check` ambiguity**: Resolved. TASK-6-6 (line 854): "the architect refers to this as a `consensus check` operation; the verb name is `consensus status`" — explicitly clarified no separate verb is created.
- **TASK-3-3 `producer_role` arg verification**: TASK-5-3 (line 691): "read `req.get('producer_role')` and `req.get('reason')` and `req.get('files_reviewed')` plus a `version` field for the proposal version reviewed". Explicit dict-arg schema, not guess-work.
- **TASK-4-1 wait set completeness**: TASK-6-1 (line 813) now lists the full set: `--for CONSENSUS_PROPOSE --for CONSENSUS_NACK --for CONSENSUS_ACK --for CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW --for STATUS --for HANDOFF --for HITL_RESOLVED --for OVERSEER_ALERT --timeout 60` — includes the previously missing `CONSENSUS_RE_REVIEW` + `HITL_RESOLVED`.
- **TASK-3-1 NACK-upward path** for memory shape: no longer needed since memory shape is now committed at plan time; preserved as runtime safety net in TASK-5-1.
- **Slice-2 sizing**: Subdivided into v2-slice-2 (schema + sync-flush + reconciliation), v2-slice-3 (next-action endpoint), v2-slice-4 (CLI verbs) per architect v3.

### Delta audit (fresh-reviewer pass)

Per the adversarial re-review mandate, I audited the v1→v2 delta as a fresh reviewer:

- **TASK-2-1/2-2/2-3 substantive durability spec** is well-grounded — `orchestrator/startup_reconciliation.py` exists (24KB), so `_startup_reconciliation_replay_safety_budget` landing there is a real seam, not a fictional file. The architecture/route citations match the existing code layout. The production failure-mode test acceptance criterion (TASK-2-6: "the **production failure-mode** test ... simulate an emptyDir wipe (clear the local working tree state, drop the local branch ref), spawn a fresh reader, assert the reader recovers the durable fields from `origin/<branch>` via `load_contract_from_branch`") is the right level of pessimism for cq-3 — it exercises the actual emptyDir-wipe failure mode the risk register R1 named, not a softer process-exit-same-FS variant.

- **TASK-3-2 `?include=durable_state` query param** introduces a coupling between slice-3 and slice-6: TASK-6-1 / TASK-6-5 / TASK-6-6 all consume `--include durable_state`. Verified internal consistency — every slice-6 task that mentions reading durable_state references back to TASK-3-2 as the source.

- **TASK-6-9 + TASK-4-5 callback loop**: TASK-6-9 (slice-6) uses `egg-contract show --field` from TASK-4-5 (slice-4). The DAG ordering (slice-4 → slice-5 → slice-6) ensures TASK-4-5 ships before TASK-6-9 is implemented. Backwards reference is valid because slice-4 is a strict ancestor.

- **TASK-6-2 OVERSEER_ALERT helper re-purposing** is now explicit: "The per-restart OVERSEER_ALERT block at lines 570-585 is **re-purposed (not duplicated)** for safety-budget exhaustion in TASK-6-5 — extract the existing helper and re-wire it; do NOT copy/paste a new alert call site." The acceptance criterion gates this: "the alert helper is the same function as the deleted-block source (re-purposed, not duplicated)." Addresses my v1 non-blocking note about R8 alert preservation.

- **TASK-6-5 host-restart re-fire suppression** (new in v2): "**Host-restart re-fire suppression** (risk_analyst NB4): if the wrapper reads `alert_emitted=true` from the durable state on startup (e.g. after host restart), the alert is NOT re-emitted — `alert_emitted` is the dedupe key." Acceptance has an explicit unit test: "A separate unit test asserts host-restart with `alert_emitted=true` in durable state does NOT re-emit OVERSEER_ALERT." Good — addresses a real risk that wasn't in my v1 NACK.

- **TASK-7-1 prior-fix preservation audit table** in `_build_brc_preamble` docstring (lines 982-985): explicitly classifies each fix (#2323/#2064/#2482/#1995/#2036/#2451/#2142/#2725) as orchestrator-enforced / CLI-enforced / prompt-only. Acceptance gates: "the function docstring contains the prior-fix audit table with each fix classified". This goes beyond what risk register R7 asked for and is the right operationalisation.

### Slice DAG and forest constraint (criterion §11 + #2137)

8 slices, single root, single linear chain with slice-7/8 as siblings under slice-6: `1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5 → 6 → {7, 8}`. Each slice has at most one parent. Forest constraint preserved. No `serialized_chain_order` needed (no diamonds).

### Trust-boundary audit (criterion §10)

- TASK-5-6 + TASK-6-11 (integration tests): correctly placed under `integration_tests/local_pipeline/` for `gateway_url` + `local_pipeline_stack` fixtures. The plan's primitives table (line 205) explicitly cites the fixture trust-boundary rule.
- TASK-2-6 (sync-flush production failure-mode test): lives under `tests/orchestrator/` (the trusted-CI-runner tier), simulates emptyDir wipe + remote ref behind directly without needing live k3s — correct execution context.
- TASK-1-3 (Qwen cache-read measurement): explicitly sourced from litellm container logs (`kubectl logs deployment/egg-litellm`), not from a path inside the agent pod. This is the trust-boundary correction the task_planner caught in v1 (the refine analysis's `~/.local/state/clm/cost-*.json` path doesn't match current code) — preserved.

### Non-blocking observations (purely informational)

- **Slice-6 still has the largest blast radius** — architect cited cq-4 atomicity in v3 and the rationale is on the record per criteria §11 final paragraph. Task_planner mirrors the architect's cite at slice-6 goal. The in-slice subdivision option (6a/6b) is documented as a fallback if implement-time produces >3 NACK rounds. Operator override on this sizing is fine.

- **TASK-4-3 conditional CLI verb** (`egg-orch brc next-action` only added if slice-3's extension proves insufficient) — well-handled. The acceptance criterion allows "not-applicable" with rationale, which is the right shape for a conditional task.

- **TASK-2-6 schemaVersion 1.2 → 1.3 bump** is now explicit (was implicit in v1). Good.

### Restate

All 4 blockers (B1, B2, B3, B4) explicitly addressed in v2 with B-tagged callouts. Delta substantively improves cq-3 durability (sync-flush + startup_reconciliation), introduces explicit host-restart re-fire suppression test, and operationalises the prior-fix preservation audit. No new blocking issues. Ready for downstream consensus.

      \ previously referenced this integration test with no owner.\"\n   Role=tester,\
      \ files=[integration_tests/local_pipeline/test_brc_memory_handler_e2e.py]. Trust-boundary\
      \ correct \u2014 uses `gateway_url` + `local_pipeline_stack` per the \xA710\

      \ pass)\n\nPer the adversarial re-review mandate, I audited the v1\u2192v2 delta\

      \ assert the reader recovers the durable fields from `origin/<branch>` via `load_contract_from_branch`\"\

      \ variant.\n\n- **TASK-3-2 `?include=durable_state` query param** introduces\
      \ a coupling between slice-3 and slice-6: TASK-6-1 / TASK-6-5 / TASK-6-6 all\
      \ consume `--include durable_state`. Verified internal consistency \u2014 every\
      \ slice-6 task that mentions reading durable_state references back to TASK-3-2\
      \ as the source.\n\n- **TASK-6-9 + TASK-4-5 callback loop**: TASK-6-9 (slice-6)\
      \ uses `egg-contract show --field` from TASK-4-5 (slice-4). The DAG ordering\
      \ (slice-4 \u2192 slice-5 \u2192 slice-6) ensures TASK-4-5 ships before TASK-6-9\
      \ is implemented. Backwards reference is valid because slice-4 is a strict ancestor.\n\

      \ criterion gates this: \"the alert helper is the same function as the deleted-block\
      \ source (re-purposed, not duplicated).\" Addresses my v1 non-blocking note\
      \ about R8 alert preservation.\n\n- **TASK-6-5 host-restart re-fire suppression**\
      \ (new in v2): \"**Host-restart re-fire suppression** (risk_analyst NB4): if\
      \ the wrapper reads `alert_emitted=true` from the durable state on startup (e.g.\
      \ after host restart), the alert is NOT re-emitted \u2014 `alert_emitted` is\

      \ test asserts host-restart with `alert_emitted=true` in durable state does\
      \ NOT re-emit OVERSEER_ALERT.\" Good \u2014 addresses a real risk that wasn't\
      \ in my v1 NACK.\n\n- **TASK-7-1 prior-fix preservation audit table** in `_build_brc_preamble`\

      the function docstring contains the prior-fix audit table with each fix classified\"\
      . This goes beyond what risk register R7 asked for and is the right operationalisation.\n\
      \n### Slice DAG and forest constraint (criterion \xA711 + #2137)\n\n8 slices,\
      \ single root, single linear chain with slice-7/8 as siblings under slice-6:\
      \ `1 \u2192 2 \u2192 3 \u2192 4 \u2192 5 \u2192 6 \u2192 {7, 8}`. Each slice\

      \ for `gateway_url` + `local_pipeline_stack` fixtures. The plan's primitives\

      \ (sync-flush production failure-mode test): lives under `tests/orchestrator/`\

      \ (Qwen cache-read measurement): explicitly sourced from litellm container logs\
      \ (`kubectl logs deployment/egg-litellm`), not from a path inside the agent\
      \ pod. This is the trust-boundary correction the task_planner caught in v1 (the\
      \ refine analysis's `~/.local/state/clm/cost-*.json` path doesn't match current\
      \ code) \u2014 preserved.\n\n### Non-blocking observations (purely informational)\n\
